\displaystyle\in\mathfrak{h}^{(\pm)}_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}},

(179)

\displaystyle\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}+}}(\Psi_{\pm}(\xi))

\displaystyle=\psi_{\pm}(P_{S_{\pm}}(\xi\oplus 0))+\psi^{{\dagger}}_{\pm}((C\oplus(-C))P_{S_{\mp}}(\xi\oplus 0)),

\displaystyle\xi

\displaystyle\in\mathfrak{h}^{(\pm)}_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}+}}.

(180)

4.2.4
Convergence of the KS approximants

In the computation of the formal scaling limit in Section
4.2.3
, we have seen that establishing convergence of one-particle operators (
166
) in a rigorous way is central to deduce the convergence of the KS approximant to the Virasoro generators in the scaling limit. Now, we make this idea precise and argue that such a result shows that
\{L^{(N)}_{k}\}_{N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
and
\{\overline{L}^{(N)}_{k}\}_{N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
are convergent sequences in the sense of Definition
3.1
with limits given by
L_{k}
and
\overline{L}_{k}
. We focus on the convergence of the chiral and anti-chiral components,
\{L^{(N)}_{\pm,k}\}_{N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
, to
L_{\pm,k}
as the convergence of the other components follows along the same line albeit the limits are the zero operators. Moreover, we show that the result extends to smeared KS approximant (
134
) and Virasoro generators (
131
).

We explicitly provide the statements for the complex fermion algebra
\mathfrak{A}_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}\cong\mathfrak{A}^{(+)}_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\mathfrak{A}^{(-)}_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}
. The statements in Majorana case
\mathfrak{B}_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}\cong\mathfrak{B}^{(+)}_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\mathfrak{B}^{(-)}_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}
are completely analogous.

Virasoro generators.

As a preparation for the convergence statement, we need to properly define the one-particle operator
\ell_{\pm,k}
of the chiral and anti-chiral Virasoro generators as unbounded operators on
\mathfrak{h}^{(\pm)}_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}\cong L^{2}(S^{1}_{L})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}
.

Definition 4.2

(One-particle Virasoro generators)
.

The operators
\ell_{\pm,k}
associated with
\mathfrak{A}^{(\pm)}_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}
and
\mathfrak{B}^{(\pm)}_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}
are given on the standard dense invariant domain
\mathcal{D}_{\std}\subset L^{2}(S^{1}_{L})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}
spanned by the plane waves
\{e_{m}\}_{m\in\Gamma_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}}
by the expressions:

\displaystyle\ell_{\pm,k}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{\pm 1}{2\pi}\sum_{l\in\Gamma_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}}(l\mp\tfrac{k}{2})e_{l\mp k}\otimes\overline{e}_{l},

(181)

where
\overline{e}_{l}
is short hand for
\langle e_{l},\!\ \cdot\!\ \rangle_{\infty}
for all
l\in\Gamma_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}
. Since we are particularly interested in associated unitary groups and,

\displaystyle\ell_{\pm,k}^{*}

\displaystyle=\ell_{\pm,-k},

(182)

holds on
\mathcal{D}_{\std}
, we define the real and imaginary parts of
\ell_{\pm,k}
,

\displaystyle r_{\pm,k}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}(\ell_{\pm,k}+\ell_{\pm,-k}),

\displaystyle\iota_{\pm,k}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2i}(\ell_{\pm,k}-\ell_{\pm,-k}),

(183)

on
\mathcal{D}_{\std}
as well.

We note that (
182
) implies that
\ell_{\pm,k}
is closable. In view of this definition, we use the following notation for the one-particle KS approximants (
145
),

\displaystyle\ell^{(N)}_{\pm,k}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{\pm 1}{2\pi}\sum_{l\in\Gamma_{N,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}}\cos(\tfrac{1}{4}\varepsilon_{N}k)^{2}\sin(\varepsilon_{N}(l\mp\tfrac{k}{2}))e_{l\mp k}\otimes\overline{e}_{l},

(184)

and for the real and imaginary parts,

\displaystyle r^{(N)}_{\pm,k}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}(\ell^{(N)}_{\pm,k}+\ell^{(N)}_{\pm,-k}),

\displaystyle\iota^{(N)}_{\pm,k}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2i}(\ell^{(N)}_{\pm,k}-\ell^{(N)}_{\pm,-k}).

(185)

as these evidently satisfy:

\displaystyle\ell^{(N)}_{\pm,k}{}^{*}

\displaystyle=\ell^{(N)}_{\pm,-k}.

(186)

The following bounds for all
j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
are straightforward:

\displaystyle\|\ell_{\pm,k}^{j}e_{m}\|_{\infty}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2L(\tfrac{L}{\pi})^{2j}\left\{\begin{matrix}|m|^{2j}&k=0\\
|k|^{2j}\left(\tfrac{\Gamma(|\frac{m}{k}|+\frac{1}{2}+j)}{\Gamma(|\frac{m}{k}|+\frac{1}{2})}\right)^{2}&k\neq 0\end{matrix}\right.,

(187)

\displaystyle \|r_{\pm,k}^{j}e_{m}\|_{\infty}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2L(\tfrac{L}{2\pi})^{2j}\left\{\begin{matrix}|2m|^{2j}&k=0\\
\binom{2j}{j}|k|^{2j}\left(\tfrac{\Gamma(|\frac{m}{k}|+\frac{1}{2}+j)}{\Gamma(|\frac{m}{k}|+\frac{1}{2})}\right)^{2}&k\neq 0\end{matrix}\right.,

with an analogous bound for
\|\iota_{\pm,k}^{j}e_{m}\|_{\infty}^{2}
. Here,
\Gamma(x+1)=x\Gamma(x)
denotes the Gamma function. These bounds imply that each plane wave
e_{m}
is an analytic vector for
\ell_{\pm,k}
,
r_{\pm,k}
and
\iota_{\pm,k}
because the series,

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\tfrac{t^{j}}{j!}\|\ell_{\pm,k}^{j}e_{m}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle<\infty,

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\tfrac{t^{j}}{j!}\|r_{\pm,k}^{j}e_{m}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle<\infty,

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\tfrac{t^{j}}{j!}\|\iota_{\pm,k}^{j}e_{m}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle<\infty,

are convergent for some
t>0
. By Nelson’s analytic vector theorem
[
70
,
74
]
we have:

Corollary 4.3
.

The plane waves
\{e_{m}\}_{m\in\Gamma_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}}\subset\mathcal{D}_{\std}
are a total set of analytic vectors for
\ell_{\pm,k}
,
r_{\pm,k}
and
\iota_{\pm,k}
. Moreover,
r_{\pm,k}
and
\iota_{\pm,k}
are essentially self-adjoint on
\mathcal{D}_{\std}
.

Because of these observations, we do not distinguish between the
\ell_{\pm,k}
,
r_{\pm,k}
,
\iota_{\pm,k}
and their closures in what follows.

Remark 4.4

(Majorana one-particle Virasoro generators)
.

In the Majorana case the one-particle Virasoro generators need to satisfy the implementability condition (
19
) which is met by the one-particle KS approximants (
184
) and the one-particle Virasoro generators (
181
):

\displaystyle C\ell^{(N)}_{\pm,k}{}^{*}C

\displaystyle=-\ell^{(N)}_{\pm,k},

\displaystyle C\ell_{\pm,k}^{*}C

\displaystyle=-\ell_{\pm,k}\;.

(188)

We are now in a position to establish the convergence of the one-particle KS approximants to the one-particle Virasoro generators in the sense of Definition
3.1
using the asymptotic maps
R^{N}_{\infty}:\ell^{2}(\Gamma_{N,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}},(2L_{N})^{-1})\cong\mathfrak{h}^{(\pm)}_{N,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}\rightarrow\mathfrak{h}^{(\pm)}_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}\cong L^{2}(S^{1}_{L})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}
, either from the wavelet or the momentum-cutoff renormalization group. In particular, we show the convergence of (
166
) in the strong operator topology on a dense, common core
\mathcal{D}\subset\mathfrak{h}^{(\pm)}_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}
, i.e.:

\displaystyle\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\|(\tilde{\ell}^{(N)}_{\pm,k}-\ell_{\pm,k})\xi\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=0,

(189)

for all
\xi\in\mathcal{D}
. A similar state holds for the real and imaginary parts (
183
) & (
185
).

For the momentum-cutoff renormalization group, we use
\mathcal{D}=\mathcal{D}_{\std}
because:

\displaystyle R^{N}_{\infty}(e_{k})

\displaystyle=\varepsilon_{N}^{-\frac{1}{2}}e_{k},

\displaystyle k

\displaystyle\in\Gamma_{N,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}\;,

(190)

where
e_{k}\in\mathfrak{h}^{(\pm)}_{N,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}
on the left but
e_{k}\in\mathfrak{h}^{(\pm)}_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}
on the right, i.e.
\bigcup_{N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}R^{N}_{\infty}(\mathfrak{h}^{(\pm)}_{N,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}})=\mathcal{D}_{\std}
.

For the wavelet renormalization group, we use
\mathcal{D}=\mathcal{D}_{W}
defined as:

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{W}=\bigcup_{N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}R^{N}_{\infty}(\mathfrak{h}^{(\pm)}_{N,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}),

(191)

which agrees with the span of the wavelet basis associated with the scaling function
s^{(\varepsilon_{0})}\in L^{2}(S^{1}_{L})_{+}
by construction
[
20
]
. That
\mathcal{D}_{W}
is dense common core for the one-particle Virasoro generators and their real and imaginary parts is due to the following argument:

We observe that the graph norms of
\ell_{\pm,k}
,
r_{\pm,k}
and
\iota_{\pm,k}
are bounded by the norm of the first-order Sobolev space
h^{1}(\Gamma_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}})
:

\displaystyle\|\xi\|_{\ell_{\pm,k}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\|\xi\|_{\infty}^{2}+\|\ell_{\pm,k}\xi\|_{\infty}^{2}\leq\max\{1+(\tfrac{Lk}{2\pi})^{2},(\tfrac{L}{\pi})^{2}\}\|\xi\|_{h^{1}(\Gamma_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}})}^{2},

and similarly for
\|\xi\|_{r_{\pm,k}}
and
\|\xi\|_{\iota_{\pm,k}}
. But, it is known that
\mathcal{D}_{\std}
and
\mathcal{D}_{W}
(provided the scaling function
s
is sufficiently regular) are dense in
h^{1}(\Gamma_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}})

[
50
]
. It follows that the graph-norm closures of
\mathcal{D}_{\std}
and
\mathcal{D}_{W}
agree, and we conclude that
\mathcal{D}_{W}
is a core for
\ell_{\pm,k}
,
r_{\pm,k}
and
\iota_{\pm,k}

[
73
]
.

Wavelet renormalization.

In this paragraph
R^{N}_{M}
and
R^{N}_{\infty}
as well as
\alpha^{N}_{M}
and
\alpha^{N}_{\infty}
denote the wavelet renormalization group of Definition
3.2
.

We formulate the essential convergence statement (
189
) as a lemma:

Lemma 4.5

(Convergence of the one-particle KS approximants)
.

Let
\xi\in D_{W}
and
s\in C^{\alpha}(\mathbb{R})
a sufficiently regular, compactly supported orthonormal scaling function. Then:

\displaystyle\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\|(\tilde{\ell}^{(N)}_{\pm,k}-\ell_{\pm,k})\xi\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=0,

where
\tilde{\ell}^{(N)}_{\pm,k}:=R^{N}_{\infty}\ell^{(N)}_{\pm,k}R^{N\!\ *}_{\infty}
, and similarly:

\displaystyle\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\|(\tilde{r}^{(N)}_{\pm,k}-r_{\pm,k})\xi\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\|(\tilde{\iota}^{(N)}_{\pm,k}-\iota_{\pm,k})\xi\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=0.

Proof.

We only spell out the proof for
\tilde{\ell}^{(N)}_{\pm,k}
and
\ell_{\pm,k}
. We first observe that:

\displaystyle\tilde{\ell}^{(N)}_{\pm,k}(R^{M}_{\infty}(\xi))

\displaystyle=\tilde{\ell}^{(N)}_{\pm,k}(R^{N}_{\infty}(R^{M}_{N}(\xi)))=R^{N}_{\infty}(\ell^{(N)}_{\pm,k}(R^{M}_{N}(\xi))),

for
N>M
and
\xi\in\mathfrak{h}_{M}
because
(R^{N}_{\infty})^{*}R^{N}_{\infty}=\1_{\mathfrak{h}_{N}}
. Now, the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality implies:

\displaystyle\|R^{N}_{\infty}(\ell^{(N)}_{\pm,k}(R^{M}_{N}(\xi)))-\ell_{\pm,k}(R^{M}_{\infty}(\xi))\|_{\infty}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\xi\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{M}}^{2}\tfrac{1}{2L}\sum_{m\in\Gamma_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}}\tfrac{1}{2L_{M}}\sum_{l\in\Gamma_{M,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}}|\underbrace{\langle e_{m},R^{N}_{\infty}(\ell^{(N)}_{\pm,k}(R^{M}_{N}(e_{l})))\rangle_{\infty}}_{:=f^{(N)}_{k,\pm}(M,m,l)}-\underbrace{\langle e_{m},\ell_{\pm,k}(R^{M}_{\infty}(e_{l}))\rangle_{\infty}}_{:=f_{k,\pm}(M,m,l)}|^{2}.

Next, we derive explicit expression for
f^{(N)}_{k,\pm}(M,m,l)
and
f_{k,\pm}(M,m,l)
to use the dominated convergence theorem with respect to
\ell^{2}(\Gamma_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}},(2L)^{-1})
to deduce the convergence of the limit
N\rightarrow\infty
:

\displaystyle f^{(N)}_{k,\pm}(M,m,l)

\displaystyle=\tfrac{\pm 1}{2\pi}\sum_{n\in\Gamma_{N,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}}\cos(\tfrac{1}{4}\varepsilon_{N}k)^{2}\sin(\varepsilon_{N}(n\mp\tfrac{k}{2}))\langle e_{m},R^{N}_{\infty}(e_{n\mp k})\rangle_{\infty}\langle e_{n},R^{M}_{N}(e_{l})\rangle_{N},

\displaystyle f_{k,\pm}(M,m,l)

\displaystyle=\tfrac{\pm 1}{2\pi}\sum_{n\in\Gamma_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}}(n\mp\tfrac{k}{2})\underbrace{\langle e_{m},e_{n\mp k}\rangle_{\infty}}_{=2L\delta_{m,n\mp k}}\langle e_{n},R^{M}_{\infty}(e_{l})\rangle_{\infty}.

Explicitly evaluating the inner product as in (
124
), this leads to:

\displaystyle f^{(N)}_{k,\pm}\!(M,m,l)

\displaystyle\!=\!\tfrac{\pm 1}{4\pi}\varepsilon_{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}\cos(\tfrac{1}{4}\varepsilon_{N}k)^{2}\hat{s}(\varepsilon_{N}m)\!\!\sum_{n\in\Gamma_{N,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}}\!\!2L_{N}\sin(\varepsilon_{N}(n\mp\tfrac{k}{2}))\delta_{0,\frac{L}{\pi}(m-(n\mp k))\!\!\!\!\mod 2L_{N}}

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt\times 2L_{M}\delta_{0,\frac{L}{\pi}(n-l)\!\!\!\!\mod 2L_{M}}\prod_{j=1}^{N-M}m_{0}(\varepsilon_{M+j}n)

\displaystyle\!=\!\tfrac{\pm 1}{4\pi}\varepsilon_{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}\cos(\tfrac{1}{4}\varepsilon_{N}k)^{2}\hat{s}(\varepsilon_{N}m)\!\!\sum_{n\in\Gamma_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}}\!\!2L_{N}\sin(\varepsilon_{N}(n\mp\tfrac{k}{2}))\delta_{0,\frac{L}{\pi}(m-(n\mp k))\!\!\!\!\mod 2L_{N}}

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt\times 2L_{M}\delta_{0,\frac{L}{\pi}(n-l)\!\!\!\!\mod 2L_{M}}\chi_{\Gamma_{N}}(n)\prod_{j=1}^{N-M}\!\!\!m_{0}(\varepsilon_{M+j}n)

\displaystyle\!=\!\tfrac{\pm L}{2\pi}2L_{M}\varepsilon_{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}\cos(\tfrac{1}{4}\varepsilon_{N}k)^{2}\hat{s}(\varepsilon_{N}m)\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\tfrac{\sin(\varepsilon_{N}(m\pm\frac{k}{2})\!)}{\varepsilon_{N}}\delta_{0,\frac{L}{\pi}(m\pm k+\frac{2\pi}{\varepsilon_{N}}n-l)\!\!\!\!\mod 2L_{M}}

\displaystyle\hskip 156.49014pt\times\!\!\chi_{\Gamma_{N}}\!(m\!\pm\!k\!+\!\tfrac{2\pi}{\varepsilon_{N}}n)\!\!\!\prod_{j=1}^{N-M}\!\!\!m_{0}(\varepsilon_{M+j}(m\!\pm\!k\!+\!\tfrac{2\pi}{\varepsilon_{N}}n)\!),

\displaystyle f_{k,\pm}\!(M,m,l)

\displaystyle\!=\!\tfrac{\pm L}{2\pi}2L_{M}\varepsilon_{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}\hat{s}(\varepsilon_{M}(m\!\pm\!k))(m\!\pm\!\tfrac{k}{2})\delta_{0,\frac{L}{\pi}(m\pm k-l)\!\!\!\!\mod 2L_{M}},

where
\chi_{\Gamma_{N}}
is the indicator function of the sublattice
\Gamma_{N,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}\subset\Gamma_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}
, and we evaluated the sum over
n\in\Gamma_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}
using
\delta_{0,\frac{L}{\pi}(m-(n\mp k))\!\!\!\!\mod 2L_{N}}
form the second to third line. Finally, we use the periodicity,
m_{0}(\varepsilon_{M+j}(m+\tfrac{2\pi}{\varepsilon_{N}}n))=m_{0}(\varepsilon_{M+j}m)
for all
m\in\Gamma_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}
,
n\in\mathbb{Z}
and
j=1,\dots,N-M
, and:

\displaystyle\delta_{0,\frac{L}{\pi}(m\pm k+\frac{2\pi}{\varepsilon_{N}}n-l)\!\!\!\!\mod 2L_{M}}

\displaystyle=\delta_{0,\frac{L}{\pi}(m\pm k-l)+2^{N-M}L_{M}n\!\!\!\!\mod 2L_{M}}=\delta_{0,\frac{L}{\pi}(m\pm k-l)\!\!\!\!\mod 2L_{M}}.

Thus, we arrive at:

\displaystyle f^{(N)}_{k,\pm}\!(M,m,l)

\displaystyle\!=\!\tfrac{\pm L}{2\pi}2L_{M}\varepsilon_{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}\cos(\tfrac{1}{4}\varepsilon_{N}k)^{2}\hat{s}(\varepsilon_{N}m)\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\tfrac{\sin(\varepsilon_{N}(m\pm\frac{k}{2}))}{\varepsilon_{N}}\delta_{0,\frac{L}{\pi}(m\pm k-l)\!\!\!\!\mod 2L_{M}}

\displaystyle\hskip 156.49014pt\times\chi_{\Gamma_{N}}\!(m\!\pm\!k\!+\!\tfrac{2\pi}{\varepsilon_{N}}n)\!\!\prod_{j=1}^{N-M}\!\!\!m_{0}(\varepsilon_{M+j}(m\!\pm\!k)\!)

\displaystyle\!=\!\tfrac{\pm L}{2\pi}2L_{M}\varepsilon_{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}\cos(\tfrac{1}{4}\varepsilon_{N}k)^{2}\hat{s}(\varepsilon_{N}m)\tfrac{\sin(\varepsilon_{N}(m\pm\frac{k}{2})\!)}{\varepsilon_{N}}\delta_{0,\frac{L}{\pi}(m\pm k-l)\!\!\!\!\mod 2L_{M}}\tfrac{\hat{s}(\varepsilon_{M}(m\pm k)\!)}{\hat{s}(\varepsilon_{N}(m\pm k)\!)},

because
\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\chi_{\Gamma_{N}}(m\pm k+\tfrac{2\pi}{\varepsilon_{N}}n)=1
for any fixed
m\pm k\in\Gamma_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}
. We also used (
102
). Next, we write:

\displaystyle f^{(N)}_{k,\pm}(M,m,l)

\displaystyle=\tfrac{\pm L}{2\pi}2L_{M}\varepsilon_{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}f^{(N)}_{\pm k}(M,m)\delta_{0,\frac{L}{\pi}(m\pm k-l)\!\!\!\!\mod 2L_{M}},

\displaystyle f_{k,\pm}(M,m,l)

\displaystyle=\tfrac{\pm L}{2\pi}2L_{M}\varepsilon_{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}f_{\pm k}(M,m)\delta_{0,\frac{L}{\pi}(m\pm k-l)\!\!\!\!\mod 2L_{M}}.

where we defined:

\displaystyle f^{(N)}_{k}(M,m)

\displaystyle:=\hat{s}(\varepsilon_{M}(m+k)\!)\cos(\tfrac{1}{4}\varepsilon_{N}k)^{2}\tfrac{\sin(\varepsilon_{N}(m+\frac{k}{2})\!)}{\varepsilon_{N}}\tfrac{\hat{s}(\varepsilon_{N}m)}{\hat{s}(\varepsilon_{N}(m+k)\!)},

\displaystyle f_{k}(M,m)

\displaystyle:=\hat{s}(\varepsilon_{M}(m+k)\!)(m\!+\!\tfrac{k}{2}).

This allows us to conclude that we have pointwise convergence (in
m\in\Gamma_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}
):

\displaystyle\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}f^{(N)}_{k}(M,m)

\displaystyle=f_{k}(M,m),

for arbitrary
M<N
and
k\in\tfrac{\pi}{L}\mathbb{Z}
, and, thus, also pointwise (in
m\in\Gamma_{\infty}
):

\displaystyle\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}f^{(N)}_{k,\pm}(M,m,l)

\displaystyle=f_{k,\pm}(M,m,l),

for arbitrary
l\in\Gamma_{M,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}
. It remains to show that
\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}f^{(N)}_{k,\pm}(M,\!\ .\!\ ,l)=f_{k,\pm}(M,\!\ .\!\ ,l)
in
\ell^{2}(\Gamma_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}},(2L)^{-1})
, which follows from the dominated convergence theorem if we provide a
g_{k}(M,\!\ .\!\ )\in\ell^{2}(\Gamma_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}},(2L)^{-1})
such that:

\displaystyle|f^{(N)}_{k}(M,m)|

\displaystyle\leq g_{k}(M,m).

The existence of such a
g_{k}(N,\!\ .\!\ )
is implied by,

\displaystyle|f^{(N)}_{k}(M,m)|

\displaystyle\leq|(m+\tfrac{k}{2})\hat{s}(\varepsilon_{M}(m+k))|\Big|\tfrac{\hat{s}(\varepsilon_{N}m)}{\hat{s}(\varepsilon_{N}(m+k)\!)}\Big|\leq C_{k}|(m+\tfrac{k}{2})\hat{s}(\varepsilon_{M}(m+k))|,

for some
C_{k}>0
, because of the uniform continuity of
\hat{s}
, and the decay estimate (
109
) for sufficiently regular
s\in C^{\alpha}(\mathbb{R})
.
\hat{s}
is uniformly continuous since
\lim_{|k|\rightarrow\infty}|\hat{s}(k)|=0
and
\hat{s}(k)=\prod_{j=1}^{\infty}m_{0}(2^{-j}k)
is locally uniformly convergent.

\Box

The lemma implies the convergence of the KS approximants in the standard Fock space representation (
c=0
).

Theorem 4.6

(Covergence of the KS approximants)
.

Let
s\in C^{\alpha}(\mathbb{R})
be a sufficiently regular, compactly supported orthonormal Daubechies scaling function. The KS approximants,
L^{(N)}_{\pm,k}
, converge strongly to the continuum Virasoro generators,
L_{\pm,k}
, on the dense, common core
\mathfrak{F}^{\alg}_{\textup{a}}(\mathcal{D}_{W})\subset\mathfrak{F}_{\textup{a}}(\mathfrak{h}^{(\pm)}_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}})
spanned by anti-symmetric Fock vectors with finitely many one-particle excitations in
\mathcal{D}_{W}
(finite
\mathcal{D}_{W}
-particle number), i.e.:

\displaystyle\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\|(\alpha^{N}_{\infty}(L^{(N)}_{\pm,k})-L_{\pm,k})a^{\dagger}(\xi_{1})\dots a^{\dagger}(\xi_{n})\Omega_{0}\|

\displaystyle=0,

(192)

for all
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
\xi_{1},\dots,\xi_{n}\in\mathcal{D}_{W}
.

Proof.

We have by the definition of second quantization:

\displaystyle dF_{0}(o)a^{\dagger}(\xi_{1})\dots a^{\dagger}(\xi_{n})\Omega_{0}

\displaystyle=\sum^{n}_{k=1}a^{\dagger}(\xi_{1})\dots a^{\dagger}(o\xi_{k})\dots a^{\dagger}(\xi_{n})\Omega_{0},

for any one-particle operator
o
on
\mathcal{D}_{o}\subset\mathfrak{h}^{(\pm)}_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}
such that
\xi_{1},...,\xi_{n}\in\mathcal{D}_{o}
. Thus, because of (
21
), we know that (
192
) is implied by (
189
).

\Box

The lemma also implies the convergence of the unitary Bogoliubov transformations associated with the KS approximants to those of the Virasoro generators (a more general statement for smeared KS approximant and Virasoro generators is stated below). Let us note that a quasi-free representation
\pi_{S}
admits an implementation of the Virasoro generators
L_{\pm,k}
by normal-ordered second quantization (
22
) provided the one-particle operators
\ell_{\pm,k}
satisfy the off-diagonal Hilbert-Schmidt bounds (
25
), for example, the representations
\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}
of the scaling limit states (
169
) & (
172
). Thus, for admissible quasi-free representations, we have:

\displaystyle\tilde{\sigma}^{(N)}_{t}

\displaystyle=\Ad_{e^{itdF_{S}(\tilde{r}^{(N)}_{\pm,k})}}(\pi_{S}(a^{\dagger}(\xi)\!)\!)=\pi_{S}(a^{\dagger}(e^{it\tilde{r}^{(N)}_{\pm,k}}\xi)\!),

(193)

\displaystyle \sigma_{t}

\displaystyle=\Ad_{e^{itdF_{S}(r_{\pm,k})}}(\pi_{S}(a^{\dagger}(\xi)\!)\!)=\pi_{S}(a^{\dagger}(e^{itr_{\pm,k}}\xi)\!),

where the unitary
e^{itdF_{S}(r_{\pm,k})}
is well-defined by essential self-adjointness of
dF_{S}(r_{\pm,k})
(see below Theorem
4.11
), and similarly for
\tilde{\iota}^{(N)}_{\pm,k}
and
\iota_{\pm,k}
by Corollary
4.3
. By construction the relation with the KS approximants is:

\displaystyle dF_{S}(\tilde{\ell}^{(N)}_{k,\pm})

\displaystyle=\ :\pi_{S}(\alpha^{N}_{\infty}(dF_{0}(\ell^{(N)}_{k,\pm}))):\ =\ :\pi_{S}(\alpha^{N}_{\infty}(L^{(N)}_{k,\pm})):.

(194)

The analogous statements hold in the Majorana setting, for example, for the scaling limit representations (
179
) & (
180
).

Corollary 4.7

(Convergence of KS Bogoliubov transformations)
.

Let
s\in C^{\alpha}(\mathbb{R})
be a sufficiently regular, compactly supported orthonormal Daubechies scaling function. In any admissible quasi-free representation
\pi_{S}
, we have:

\displaystyle\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\|\tilde{\sigma}^{(N)}_{t}(\pi_{S}(A))-\sigma_{t}(\pi_{S}(A))\|

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\forall A\in\mathfrak{A}^{(\pm)}_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}},

and uniformly on compact intervals in
t\in\mathbb{R}
.

Proof.

It is sufficient to prove the statement for
a=a^{\dagger}(\xi)
with
\xi\in\mathfrak{h}^{(\pm)}_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}
, because the multinomials
A=a^{\dagger}(\xi_{1})\dots a^{\dagger}(\xi_{n})a(\xi_{n+1})\dots a(\xi_{n+m})
form a norm total set in
\mathfrak{A}^{(\pm)}_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}
and we have:

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\sigma}^{(N)}_{t}(AB)-\sigma_{t}(AB)\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\tilde{\sigma}^{(N)}_{t}(A)-\sigma_{t}(A)\|\|B\|+\|A\|\|\tilde{\sigma}^{(N)}_{t}(B)-\sigma_{t}(B)\|,

for all
A,B\in\pi_{S}(\mathfrak{A}^{(\pm)}_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}})
. Now, let
o=r_{\pm,k},\iota_{\pm,k}
and
\tilde{o}^{(N)}=r^{(N)}_{\pm,k},\tilde{\iota}^{(N)}_{\pm,k}
. Then, because of (
193
), we have:

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\sigma}^{(N)}_{t}(\pi_{S}(a^{\dagger}(\xi)))-\sigma_{t}(\pi_{S}(a^{\dagger}(\xi)))\|

\displaystyle=\|\pi_{S}(a^{\dagger}(e^{it\tilde{o}^{(N)}}\xi))-\pi_{S}(a^{\dagger}(e^{ito}\xi))\|

\displaystyle\leq\|(e^{it\tilde{o}^{(N)}}-e^{ito})\xi\|_{\infty}.

Now,
\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\|(e^{it\tilde{o}^{(N)}}-e^{ito})\xi\|_{\infty}=0
uniformly on compact intervals in
t\in\mathbb{R}
because
\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\|(\tilde{o}^{(N)}-o)\xi\|_{\infty}=0
for all
\xi\in\mathcal{D}_{W}
by Lemma
4.5
which entails strong resolvent convergence
[
73
]
.

\Box

Remark 4.8
.

For multinomials
A=a^{\dagger}(\xi_{1})\dots a^{\dagger}(\xi_{n})a(\xi_{n+1})\dots a(\xi_{n+m})
we have the estimate:

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\sigma}^{(N)}_{t}(\pi_{S}(A))-\sigma_{t}(\pi_{S}(A))\|

\displaystyle\leq\left(\prod_{l=1}^{n+m}\|\xi_{l}\|_{\infty}\right)\sum_{k=1}^{n+m}\frac{\|(e^{it\tilde{o}^{(N)}}-e^{ito})\xi_{k}\|_{\infty}}{\|\xi_{k}\|_{\infty}}.

(195)

Corollary 4.9

(Convergence of KS Bogoliubov derivations)
.

With the same assumptions and notation as in Corollary
4.7
, we have the convergence of the quasi-free derivations,

\displaystyle\tfrac{d}{dt}_{|t=0}\tilde{\sigma}^{(N)}_{t}(\pi_{S}(a(\xi)))

\displaystyle=i[:\!dF_{S}(\tilde{o}^{(N)})\!:,a(\xi)]=a(i\tilde{o}^{(N)}\xi),

\displaystyle\tfrac{d}{dt}_{|t=0}\sigma_{t}(\pi_{S}(a(\xi)))

\displaystyle=i[:\!dF_{S}(o)\!:,a(\xi)]=a(io\xi),

for
A\in\mathfrak{A}^{(\pm)}_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}
in the algebraic span of
a^{{\dagger}}(\xi),a(\xi)
with
\xi\in\mathcal{D}_{W}
. Moreover, we have:

\displaystyle\|[:\!dF_{S}(\tilde{o}^{(N)})\!:,A]-[:\!dF_{S}(o)\!:,A]\|

\displaystyle\leq\left(\prod_{l=1}^{n+m}\|\xi_{l}\|_{\infty}\right)\sum_{k=1}^{n+m}\frac{\|(\tilde{o}^{(N)}-o)\xi_{k}\|_{\infty}}{\|\xi_{k}\|_{\infty}}\;

(196)

for
A=a^{\dagger}(\xi_{1})\dots a^{\dagger}(\xi_{n})a(\xi_{n+1})\dots a(\xi_{n+m})
.

Momentum-cutoff renormalization.

The convergence results for the wavelet renormalization group lead to the convergence of the KS approximants to the Virasoro generators in the standard Fock representation (
c=0
). Clearly, we would like to achieve a similar statement for non-vanishing central charge (
c\neq 0
). Specifically, we would like to deduce the convergence of the KS approximants in the representations
\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}
of the scaling limit states (
169
) & (
172
) as well as (
179
) & (
180
). Such a result is achieved by using the momentum-cutoff renormalization group of Definition
3.9
. Although, we initially lose the compatibility with the quasi-local structure (see Proposition
3.6
) in this way, we explain in the next paragraph of this subsection how to remedy this issue by combining the wavelet renormalization group with the momentum-cutoff renormalization group for smeared KS approximants and Virasoro generators.

Moreover, it is necessary to slightly modify the KS approximants (
184
),

\displaystyle\ell^{(N)}_{k,\pm}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{\pm 1}{2\pi}\sum_{l\in\Gamma_{N,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}}\cos(\tfrac{1}{4}\varepsilon_{N}k)^{2}\sin(\varepsilon_{N}(l\mp\tfrac{k}{2}))\chi_{\Gamma_{N}}(l\mp k)e_{l\mp k}\otimes\overline{e}_{l}\;,

(197)

where the additional factor
\chi_{\Gamma_{N}}
has been introduced to avoid spurious contributions due to the periodic boundary conditions for one-particle functions on the dual momentum-space lattice
\Gamma_{N}
. As the scale
N
can be chosen large compared to intrinsic scale of any fixed
k\in\Gamma_{M,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}+}}
, i.e.,
N\gg M
, this modification does not affect the asymptotic properties of
\ell^{(N)}_{k,\pm}
. A direct computation shows that the modified KS approximants still satisfy the adjointness relations (
186
) and implementability conditions (
188
)
15
15

15

In the Ramond sector some additional care is necessary, i.e.,
\chi_{\Gamma_{N}}
needs to be defined as
\chi_{(-\frac{\pi}{\varepsilon_{N}},\frac{\pi}{\varepsilon_{N}})\cap\Gamma_{N}}
to ensure is invariance under reflections about
0
.
. We note that the modification of the KS approximants at the one-particle level can also be translated into modification of the formula of the KS approximants in Definition
4.1
.

Denoting by
R^{N}_{M}
and
R^{N}_{\infty}
as well as
\alpha^{N}_{M}
and
\alpha^{N}_{\infty}
the momentum-cutoff renormalization group throughout this paragraph, the one-particle operators
\tilde{\ell}^{(N)}_{k,\pm}
are now given by:

\displaystyle\tilde{\ell}^{(N)}_{k,\pm}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{\pm 1}{2\pi}\sum_{l\in\Gamma_{N,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}}\cos(\tfrac{1}{4}\varepsilon_{N}k)^{2}\tfrac{\sin(\varepsilon_{N}(l\mp\frac{k}{2}))}{\varepsilon_{N}}\chi_{\Gamma_{N}}(l\mp k)e_{l\mp k}\otimes\overline{e}_{l}\;.

(198)

To prove the analogue of Theorem
4.6
for
\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}
(with
c=1,\tfrac{1}{2}
), we need to compare the matrix elements of
\tilde{\ell}^{(N)}_{\pm,k}
with those of
\ell_{\pm,k}
:

\displaystyle(\tilde{\ell}^{(N)}_{\pm,k})_{mn}

\displaystyle\!=\!\pm\tfrac{L}{\pi}2L\delta_{m,n\mp k}\cos(\tfrac{1}{4}\varepsilon_{N}k)^{2}\tfrac{\sin(\varepsilon_{N}(n\mp\frac{k}{2}))}{\varepsilon_{N}}\chi_{\Gamma_{N}(n\!\mp\!k)}\chi_{\Gamma_{N}}(n),

(199)

\displaystyle (\ell_{\pm,k})_{mn}

\displaystyle\!=\!\pm\tfrac{L}{\pi}2L\delta_{m,n\mp k}(n\!\mp\!\tfrac{k}{2}).

If we have an approximating sequence
\{o^{(N)}\}_{N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
of
o
on the one-particle space
\mathfrak{h}^{(\pm)}_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}
, the convergence of their normal-ordered second quantizations can be analyzed using the following estimate because of (
16
) & (
25
):

\displaystyle\|dF_{S}(\tilde{o}^{(N)}\!-\!o)a^{{\dagger}}\!(R^{M}_{\infty}(\eta_{1})\!)...a^{{\dagger}}\!(R^{M}_{\infty}(\eta_{n})\!)\Omega_{0}\|

\displaystyle\!\leq\!\!\Big(\!\sum_{k=1}^{n}\!\Big\{\!\tfrac{\|(\tilde{o}^{(N)}-o)_{++}R^{M}_{\infty}(\eta_{k})\|_{\infty}+\|(\tilde{o}^{(N)}-o)^{t}_{--}R^{M}_{\infty}(\eta_{k})\|_{\infty}}{\|\eta_{k}\|_{M}}\!\Big\}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\!(n\!+\!2)\|(\tilde{o}^{(N)}\!-\!o)_{+-}\|_{2}\!+\!n\|(\tilde{o}^{(N)}\!-\!o)_{-+}\|_{2}\!\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\times\prod_{l=1}^{n}\|\eta_{l}\|_{M}.

(200)

For
\tilde{o}^{(N)}=\tilde{\ell}^{(N)}_{\pm,k}
and
o=\ell_{\pm,k}
, we find (
N>M
):

\displaystyle\|((\tilde{\ell}^{(N)}_{k,\pm}-\ell_{k,\pm})_{\pm\pm})^{(t)}R^{M}_{\infty}(\eta)\|_{\infty}^{2}

(201)

\displaystyle\leq\|\eta\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{M}}^{2}(\tfrac{L}{\pi})^{2}\!\!\!\sum_{n\in\Gamma_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}}\!\!\!\chi_{\Gamma_{M}}(n)\theta(\pm(n\!\mp\!k))\theta(\pm n)\Big|\!\cos(\tfrac{1}{4}\varepsilon_{N}k)^{2}\tfrac{\sin(\varepsilon_{N}(n\mp\frac{k}{2}))}{\varepsilon_{N}}\chi_{\Gamma_{N}(n\!\mp\!k)}\!-\!(n\!\mp\!\tfrac{k}{2})\Big|^{2},

\displaystyle\|(\tilde{\ell}^{(N)}_{k,\pm}-\ell_{k,\pm})_{\pm\mp}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq(\tfrac{L}{\pi})^{2}\!\!\!\sum_{n\in\Gamma_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}}\!\!\theta(\pm(n\!\mp\!k))\theta(\mp n)\Big|\cos(\tfrac{1}{4}\varepsilon_{N}k)^{2}\tfrac{\sin(\varepsilon_{N}(n\mp\frac{k}{2}))}{\varepsilon_{N}}\chi_{\Gamma_{N}(n\!\mp\!k)}\chi_{\Gamma_{N}}(n)\!-\!(n\!\mp\!\tfrac{k}{2})\Big|^{2}.

The sums in these estimates are finite (for fixed
M
) which entails convergence for
N\rightarrow\infty
. It is evident from (
201
), that we have the analogue of Lemma
4.5
:

Lemma 4.10
.

Let
\xi\in D_{\std}
. Then:

\displaystyle\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\|(\tilde{\ell}^{(N)}_{\pm,k}\!-\!\ell_{\pm,k})\xi\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\|(\tilde{\ell}^{(N)}_{\pm,k}\!-\!\ell_{\pm,k})_{\pm\pm}\xi\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=0,\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\|(\tilde{\ell}^{(N)}_{\pm,k}\!-\!\ell_{\pm,k})_{\pm\mp}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=0,

where
\tilde{\ell}^{(N)}_{\pm,k}:=R^{N}_{\infty}\ell^{(N)}_{\pm,k}R^{N\!\ *}_{\infty}
, and similarly:

\displaystyle\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\|(\tilde{r}^{(N)}_{\pm,k}\!-\!r_{\pm,k})\xi\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\|(\tilde{r}^{(N)}_{\pm,k}\!-\!r_{\pm,k})_{\pm\mp}\xi\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\|(\tilde{r}^{(N)}_{\pm,k}\!-\!r_{\pm,k})_{\pm\mp}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\|(\tilde{\iota}^{(N)}_{\pm,k}\!-\!\iota_{\pm,k})\xi\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\|(\tilde{\iota}^{(N)}_{\pm,k}\!-\!\iota_{\pm,k})_{\pm\pm}\xi\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\|(\tilde{\iota}^{(N)}_{\pm,k}\!-\!\iota_{\pm,k})_{\pm\mp}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=0.

Because of Remark
4.4
, similar estimates as (
4.2.4
) and (
201
) also hold for
dQ_{S}
in Majorana setting. Applying the lemma, we have:

Theorem 4.11

(Covergence of the KS approximants for
\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}
)
.

The KS approximants,
L^{(N)}_{\pm,k}
, converge strongly to the continuum Virasoro generators,
L_{\pm,k}
, on the dense, common core
\mathfrak{F}^{\alg}_{\textup{a}}(\mathcal{D}_{\std})\subset\mathfrak{F}_{\textup{a}}(\mathfrak{h}^{(\pm)}_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}})
spanned by anti-symmetric Fock vectors with finitely many one-particle excitations in
\mathcal{D}_{\std}
(finite
\mathcal{D}_{\std}
-particle number):

\displaystyle\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\|(:\!\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}(\alpha^{N}_{\infty}(L^{(N)}_{\pm,k})\!)\!:-L_{\pm,k})a^{\dagger}(\xi_{1})\dots a^{\dagger}(\xi_{n})\Omega_{0}\|

\displaystyle=0,

(202)

for all
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
\xi_{1},\dots,\xi_{n}\in\mathcal{D}_{\std}
.

By Corollary
4.3
and (
4.2.4
),
\mathfrak{F}^{\alg}_{\textup{a}}(\mathcal{D}_{\std})
contains a total set of analytic vectors for
L_{\pm,k}
, and by Nelson’s analytic vector theorem
[
70
]
the real and imaginary parts of
L_{\pm,k}
are essentially self-adjoint on
\mathfrak{F}^{\alg}_{\textup{a}}(\mathcal{D}_{\std})
. This implies strong resolvent convergence of the KS approximants and, therefore
[
73
]
:

Corollary 4.12

(Convergence of KS unitaries)
.

Consider the unitaries,

\displaystyle U^{(N)}_{t}

\displaystyle=e^{itdF_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}(\tilde{o}^{(N)})},

\displaystyle U_{t}

\displaystyle=e^{itdF_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}(o)},

for
o^{(N)}=\tilde{r}^{(N)}_{\pm,k},\tilde{\iota}^{(N)}_{\pm,k}
and
o=r_{\pm,k},\iota_{\pm,k}
. Then,

\displaystyle\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\|(U^{(N)}_{t}-U_{t})\phi\|

\displaystyle=0,

for all
\phi\in\mathfrak{F}_{\textup{a}}(\mathfrak{h}^{(\pm)}_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}})
uniformly on compact intervals in
t\in\mathbb{R}
.

As in the previous section, Lemma
4.10
implies the following convergence statement for the Bogoliubov transformations (
193
):

Corollary 4.13

(Convergence of KS Bogoliubov transformations for
\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}
)
.

In the quasi-free representations
\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}
, we have:

\displaystyle\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\|\tilde{\sigma}^{(N)}_{t}(\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}(A))-\sigma_{t}(\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}(A))\|

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\forall A\in\mathfrak{A}^{(\pm)}_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}},

and uniformly on compact intervals in
t\in\mathbb{R}
.

Remark 4.14

(Moebius group in the Neveu-Schwarz sector)
.

We note that the Hilbert-Schmidt norms in (
201
) vanish in the Neveu-Schwarz sector ((
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}
)-boundary condition) for
k=0,\pm\tfrac{\pi}{L}
, i.e.
\|(\tilde{\ell}^{(N)}_{k,\pm}-\ell_{k,\pm})_{\pm\mp}\|_{2}^{2}=0
for the approximation of the Virasoro generators corresponding to the Moebius group. Therefore, a simple extension of Lemma
4.5
is sufficient to prove Theorem
4.11
in this case using the wavelet renormalization group.

Smeared KS approximants.

We combine the wavelet and momentum-cutoff renormalization groups to analyze the convergence of the smeared KS approximants (
134
) to the smeared Virasoro generators (
131
). In this way, it is possible to exploit the convergence of the KS approximants in the non-trivial quasi-free representations
\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}
while, at the same time, preserving localization in real space in the sense of Proposition
3.6
.

Throughout this paragraph we denote by
R^{N}_{M}
and
R^{N}_{\infty}
as well as
\alpha^{N}_{M}
and
\alpha^{N}_{\infty}
the momentum-cutoff renormalization group, while
S^{N}_{M}
and
S^{N}_{\infty}
denote the wavelet renormalization group for differential loops (see Definition
3.4
).

Lemma 4.15

(Convergence of smeared one-particle KS approximants)
.

Let
s\in C^{\alpha}(\mathbb{R})
be a sufficiently regular, compactly supported orthonormal Daubechies scaling function. For
M\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
X\in\mathfrak{l}(\Lambda_{M},\mathbb{C})
, we consider,

\displaystyle\tilde{\ell}^{(N)}_{\pm}(S^{M}_{N}(X))

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2L_{N}}\sum_{k\in\Gamma_{N,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}+}}}S^{M}_{N}(\hat{X})_{k}\tilde{\ell}^{(N)}_{\pm,k},

\displaystyle\ell_{\pm}(S^{M}_{\infty}(X))

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2L}\sum_{k\in\Gamma_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}+}}}S^{M}_{\infty}(\hat{X})_{k}\ell_{\pm,k},

for any
N\geq M
.Then,

\displaystyle\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\|(\tilde{\ell}^{(N)}_{\pm}(S^{M}_{N}(X)\!)\!-\!\ell_{\pm}(S^{M}_{\infty}(X)\!)\!)_{\pm\pm}\xi\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\|(\tilde{\ell}^{(N)}_{\pm}(S^{M}_{N}(X)\!)\!-\!\ell_{\pm}(S^{M}_{\infty}(X)\!)\!)\xi\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=0,

for
\xi\in\mathcal{D}_{\std}
, and,

\displaystyle\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\|(\tilde{\ell}^{(N)}_{\pm}(S^{M}_{N}(X)\!)\!-\!\ell_{\pm}(S^{M}_{\infty}(X)\!)\!)_{\pm\mp}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=0.

Proof.

For sufficiently regular
s
, we know that,

\displaystyle\ell_{\pm}(S^{M}_{\infty}(X))

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2L_{M}}\sum_{k\in\Gamma_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}+}}}\hat{s}(\varepsilon_{M}k)\hat{X}_{\per|k}\ell_{\pm,k},

is well-defined on
\mathcal{D}_{\std}
because of (
108
). Now, we use,

\displaystyle\tilde{\ell}^{(N)}_{\pm}(S^{M}_{N}(X)\!)\!-\!\ell_{\pm}(S^{M}_{\infty}(X)\!)

\displaystyle\!=\!\tilde{\ell}^{(N)}_{\pm}(S^{M}_{N}(X)\!-\!\chi_{\Gamma_{N}}S^{M}_{\infty}(X)\!)\!+\!\tilde{\ell}^{(N)}_{\pm}(\chi_{\Gamma_{N}}S^{M}_{\infty}(X)\!)\!-\!\ell_{\pm}(S^{M}_{\infty}(X)\!),

to write:

\displaystyle\|(\tilde{\ell}^{(N)}_{\pm}(S^{M}_{N}(X)\!)\!-\!\ell_{\pm}(S^{M}_{\infty}(X)\!)\!)_{\pm\pm}\xi\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\!\leq\!\|\tilde{\ell}^{(N)}_{\pm}(S^{M}_{N}(X)\!-\!\chi_{\Gamma_{N}}S^{M}_{\infty}(X)\!)_{\pm\pm}\xi\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\|(\tilde{\ell}^{(N)}_{\pm}(\chi_{\Gamma_{N}}S^{M}_{\infty}(X)\!)\!-\!\ell_{\pm}(S^{M}_{\infty}(X)\!)\!)_{\pm\pm}\xi\|_{\infty},

\displaystyle\|(\tilde{\ell}^{(N)}_{\pm}(S^{M}_{N}(X)\!)\!-\!\ell_{\pm}(S^{M}_{\infty}(X)\!)\!)\xi\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\!\leq\!\|\tilde{\ell}^{(N)}_{\pm}(S^{M}_{N}(X)\!-\!\chi_{\Gamma_{N}}S^{M}_{\infty}(X)\!)\xi\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\|(\tilde{\ell}^{(N)}_{\pm}(\chi_{\Gamma_{N}}S^{M}_{\infty}(X)\!)\!-\!\ell_{\pm}(S^{M}_{\infty}(X)\!)\!)\xi\|_{\infty},

\displaystyle\|(\tilde{\ell}^{(N)}_{\pm}(S^{M}_{N}(X)\!)\!-\!\ell_{\pm}(S^{M}_{\infty}(X)\!)\!)_{\pm\mp}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\!\leq\!\|\tilde{\ell}^{(N)}_{\pm}(S^{M}_{N}(X)\!-\!\chi_{\Gamma_{N}}S^{M}_{\infty}(X)\!)_{\pm\mp}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\|(\tilde{\ell}^{(N)}_{\pm}(\chi_{\Gamma_{N}}S^{M}_{\infty}(X)\!)\!-\!\ell_{\pm}(S^{M}_{\infty}(X)\!)\!)_{\pm\mp}\|_{2}

Now, let
\xi=R^{K}_{\infty}(\eta)
for
\eta\in\mathfrak{h}_{K}
. By the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality we have:

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\ell}^{(N)}_{\pm}(S^{M}_{N}(X)\!-\!\chi_{\Gamma_{N}}S^{M}_{\infty}(X)\!)_{\pm\pm}R^{K}_{\infty}(\eta)\|_{\infty}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\eta\|_{K}^{2}\tfrac{1}{2L_{K}}\!\!\!\sum_{l\in\Gamma_{K,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}}\!\|\tfrac{1}{2L}\!\!\sum_{k\in\Gamma_{N,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}+}}}\!\!\varepsilon_{M}\Bigg(\prod_{j=1}^{N-M}m_{0}(\varepsilon_{M+j}k)\!-\!\hat{s}(\varepsilon_{M}k)\Bigg)\hat{X}_{\per|k}(\tilde{\ell}^{(N)}_{\pm,k})_{\pm\pm}R^{K}_{\infty}(e_{l})\|_{\infty}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\eta\|_{K}^{2}\tfrac{1}{2L_{K}}\!\!\!\sum_{l\in\Gamma_{K,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}}\!\!\!\Bigg(\tfrac{1}{2L}\!\!\sum_{k\in\Gamma_{N,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}+}}}\!\!\!\big(1\!+\!|\varepsilon_{M}k|\big)^{2\delta}\Big|\varepsilon_{M}\Bigg(\!\prod_{j=1}^{N-M}m_{0}(\varepsilon_{M+j}k)\!-\!\hat{s}(\varepsilon_{M}k)\!\Bigg)\hat{X}_{\per|k}\Big|^{2}\!\Bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip 68.28644pt\times\Bigg(\!\tfrac{1}{2L}\!\!\sum_{k\in\Gamma_{N,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}+}}}\!\!\!\big(1\!+\!|\varepsilon_{M}k|\big)^{-2\delta}\|(\tilde{\ell}^{(N)}_{\pm,k})_{\pm\pm}R^{K}_{\infty}(e_{l})\|_{\infty}^{2}\!\!\Bigg),

and,

\displaystyle\|(\tilde{\ell}^{(N)}_{\pm}(\chi_{\Gamma_{N}}S^{M}_{\infty}(X)\!)\!-\!\ell_{\pm}(S^{M}_{\infty}(X)\!)\!)_{\pm\pm}R^{K}_{\infty}(\eta)\|_{\infty}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\eta\|_{K}^{2}\tfrac{1}{2L_{K}}\!\!\!\sum_{l\in\Gamma_{K,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}}\!\|\tfrac{1}{2L}\!\!\sum_{k\in\Gamma_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}+}}}\!\!\varepsilon_{M}\hat{s}(\varepsilon_{M}k)\hat{X}_{\per|k}(\chi_{\Gamma_{N}}(k)(\tilde{\ell}^{(N)}_{\pm,k})\!-\!(\ell_{\pm,k})\!)_{\pm\pm}R^{K}_{\infty}(e_{l})\|_{\infty}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\eta\|_{K}^{2}\tfrac{1}{2L_{K}}\!\!\!\sum_{l\in\Gamma_{K,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}}\!\!\!\Bigg(\tfrac{1}{2L}\!\!\sum_{k\in\Gamma_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}+}}}\!\!\!\big(1\!+\!|\varepsilon_{M}k|\big)^{2\delta}\Big|\varepsilon_{M}\hat{s}(\varepsilon_{M}k)\hat{X}_{\per|k}\Big|^{2}\!\Bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip 68.28644pt\times\Bigg(\!\tfrac{1}{2L}\!\!\sum_{k\in\Gamma_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}+}}}\!\!\!\big(1\!+\!|\varepsilon_{M}k|\big)^{-2\delta}\|(\chi_{\Gamma_{N}}(k)(\tilde{\ell}^{(N)}_{\pm,k})\!-\!(\ell_{\pm,k})\!)_{\pm\pm}R^{K}_{\infty}(e_{l})\|_{\infty}^{2}\!\!\Bigg).

For sufficiently large
\delta>0
and
\alpha>0
, this implies the first convergence statement because of (
201
), Lemma
3.7
, and the polynomial boundedness of
\|\tilde{\ell}^{(N)}_{\pm,k}R^{K}_{\infty}(e_{l})\|_{\infty}
and
\|\ell_{\pm,k}R^{K}_{\infty}(e_{l})\|_{\infty}
in
k\in\Gamma_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}
due to (
187
).

The case without the restriction to the diagonal parts
(\!\ \cdot\!\ )_{\pm\pm}
of the block-diagonal decomposition (
24
) is analogous. Similarly, we find:

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\ell}^{(N)}_{\pm}\!(S^{M}_{N}\!(X)\!-\!\chi_{\Gamma_{N}}S^{M}_{\infty}\!(X)\!)_{\pm\mp}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\!\leq\!\!\Bigg(\!\!\tfrac{1}{2L}\!\!\!\!\sum_{k\in\Gamma_{N,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}+}}}\!\!\!\!\!\big(1\!+\!|\varepsilon_{M}k|\big)^{2\delta}\!\varepsilon_{M}\Big|\!\!\!\!\prod_{j=1}^{\ N-M}\!\!\!\!m_{0}(\varepsilon_{M+j}k)\!-\!\hat{s}(\varepsilon_{M}k)\Big|^{2}\!|\hat{X}_{\per|k}|^{2}\!\Bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\times\Bigg(\!\tfrac{1}{2L}\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{k\in\Gamma_{N,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}}\!\!\!\big(1\!+\!|\varepsilon_{M}k|\big)^{-2\delta}\|(\tilde{\ell}^{(N)}_{\pm,k})_{\pm\mp}\|_{2}^{2}\!\!\Bigg),

and,

\displaystyle\|(\tilde{\ell}^{(N)}_{\pm}(\chi_{\Gamma_{N}}S^{M}_{\infty}(X)\!)\!-\!\ell_{\pm}(S^{M}_{\infty}(X)\!)\!)_{\pm\mp}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\!\leq\!\!\Bigg(\tfrac{1}{2L}\!\!\sum_{k\in\Gamma_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}+}}}\!\!\!\big(1\!+\!|\varepsilon_{M}k|\big)^{2\delta}\Big|\varepsilon_{M}\hat{s}(\varepsilon_{M}k)\hat{X}_{\per|k}\Big|^{2}\!\Bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip 7.11317pt\times\!\!\Bigg(\!\tfrac{1}{2L}\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{k\in\Gamma_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}+}}}\!\!\!\!\!\big(1\!+\!|\varepsilon_{M}k|\big)^{-2\delta}\|(\chi_{\Gamma_{N}}\!(k)\tilde{\ell}^{(N)}_{\pm,k}\!-\!\ell_{\pm,k})_{\pm\mp}\|_{2}^{2}\!\!\Bigg).

Again for sufficiently large
\delta>0
and
\alpha>0
, the convergence of the second statement follows using (
201
), the observation that
\|(\tilde{\ell}^{(N)}_{\pm,k})_{\pm\mp}\|_{2}
and
\|(\ell_{\pm,k})_{\pm\mp}\|_{2}
are polynomially bounded in
k\in\Gamma_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}
:

\displaystyle\|(\tilde{\ell}^{(N)}_{\pm,k})_{\pm\mp}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=(\tfrac{L}{\pi})^{2}\!\!\!\!\sum_{n\in\Gamma_{N,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}}\!\!\!\!\theta(\pm n\!-\!k)\theta(\mp n)\cos(\tfrac{1}{4}\varepsilon_{N}k)^{4}\Big(\tfrac{\sin(\varepsilon_{N}(n\mp\frac{k}{2}))}{\varepsilon_{N}}\Big)^{2}\chi_{\Gamma_{N}(n\!\mp\!k)},

\displaystyle\|(\ell_{\pm,k})_{\pm\mp}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=(\tfrac{L}{\pi})^{2}\!\!\!\!\sum_{n\in\Gamma_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}}\!\!\!\!\theta(\pm n\!-\!k)\theta(\mp n)(n\!\mp\!\tfrac{k}{2})^{2}.

\Box

As before, we use the preceding lemma to deduce the following result on the convergence of the KS approximants (cf. (
4.2.4
)).

Theorem 4.16

(Convergence of smeared KS approximants for
\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}
)
.

Let
s\in C^{\alpha}(\mathbb{R})
be a sufficiently regular, compactly supported orthonormal Daubechies scaling function, and let
X\in\mathfrak{l}(\Lambda_{M},\mathbb{C})
for some
M\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Then, the smeared KS approximants,
L^{(N)}_{\pm}(S^{M}_{N}(X))
, converge strongly to the continuum Virasoro generators,
L_{\pm}(S^{M}_{\infty}(X))
, on the dense domain
\mathfrak{F}^{\alg}_{\textup{a}}(\mathcal{D}_{\std})\subset\mathfrak{F}_{\textup{a}}(\mathfrak{h}^{(\pm)}_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}})
spanned by anti-symmetric Fock vectors with finitely many one-particle excitations in
\mathcal{D}_{\std}
(finite
\mathcal{D}_{\std}
-particle number):

\displaystyle\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\|(:\!\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}(\alpha^{N}_{\infty}(L^{(N)}_{\pm}(S^{M}_{N}(X)))\!)\!:-L_{\pm}(S^{M}_{\infty}(X)))a^{\dagger}(\xi_{1})\dots a^{\dagger}(\xi_{n})\Omega_{0}\|

\displaystyle=0,

(203)

for all
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
\xi_{1},\dots,\xi_{n}\in\mathcal{D}_{\std}
.

By the results in
[
15
]
,
\mathfrak{F}^{\alg}_{\textup{a}}(\mathcal{D}_{\std})
is a domain of essential self-adjointness of
L_{\pm}(X)
for real smearing functions
X\in\mathfrak{l}^{\frac{3}{2}}(S^{1},\mathbb{R})
(the representations
\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}
have positive energy and
c>0
, see also
[
41
]
). Thus, the preceding theorem implies strong resolvent convergence of the smeared KS approximants and, therefore
[
73
]
:

Corollary 4.17

(Convergence of smeared KS unitaries)
.

For
X\in\mathfrak{l}(\Lambda_{M},\mathbb{R})
, consider the unitaries,

\displaystyle U^{(N)}_{t}(S^{M}_{N}(X))

\displaystyle=e^{it:\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}(\alpha^{N}_{\infty}(L^{(N)}_{\pm}(S^{M}_{N}(X)))):},

\displaystyle U_{t}(X)

\displaystyle=e^{itL_{\pm}(S^{M}_{\infty}(X))},

Then,

\displaystyle\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\|(U^{(N)}_{t}(X)-U_{t}(X))\phi\|

\displaystyle=0,

for all
\phi\in\mathfrak{F}_{\textup{a}}(\mathfrak{h}^{(\pm)}_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}})
uniformly on compact intervals in
t\in\mathbb{R}
.

Remark 4.18
.

We can lift the restriction to sequences
\{S^{M}_{N}(X)\}_{N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
with
X\in\mathfrak{l}(\Lambda_{M},\mathbb{C})
(called basic sequences in
[
22
]
) by:

\displaystyle\tilde{\ell}^{(N)}_{\pm}(X_{N})\!-\!\ell_{\pm}(X)

\displaystyle\!=\!\tilde{\ell}^{(N)}_{\pm}(X_{N}\!-\!S^{M}_{N}(X_{M})\!)\!+\!\tilde{\ell}^{(N)}_{\pm}(S^{M}_{N}(X_{M})\!)\!-\!\ell_{\pm}(S^{M}_{\infty}(X)\!)\!+\!\ell_{\pm}(S^{M}_{\infty}(X)\!-\!X),

for
X_{N}\in\mathfrak{l}(\Lambda_{N},\mathbb{C})
and
X\in\mathfrak{l}^{\alpha}(S^{1}_{L},\mathbb{C})
(
\alpha>0
). Then, assuming we have a
S
-convergent sequence of smearing functions
X_{N}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle N\rightarrow\infty}}{{\rightarrow}}X
in the sense of Definition
3.1
for a suitable sequence of Sobolev-type semi-norm
\{p_{N,\delta}\}_{N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
,

\displaystyle\lim_{M\rightarrow\infty}\limsup_{N\rightarrow\infty}p_{N,\delta}(X_{N}-S^{M}_{N}(X_{M})\!)

\displaystyle=0,

we conclude the convergence of the KS approximants as in (
203
). In principle this allows us to reconstruct the smeared Virasoro generators for smooth smearing functions
X\in\mathfrak{l}^{\infty}(S^{1}_{L},\mathbb{C})
.

In analogy with the Corollaries
4.7
&
4.13
, Lemma
4.15
implies the convergence of the Bogoliubov transformation with smeared generators, see (
137
):

Corollary 4.19

(Convergence of smeared KS Bogoliubov transformations)
.

Let
s\in C^{\alpha}(\mathbb{R})
be a sufficiently regular, compactly supported orthonormal Daubechies scaling function. In the quasi-free representations
\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}
, we have:

\displaystyle\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\|\tilde{\sigma}^{(N)}_{tS^{M}_{N}(X)}(\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}(A))-\sigma_{tS^{M}_{\infty}(X)}(\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}(A))\|

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\forall A\in\mathfrak{A}^{(\pm)}_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}},

uniformly on compact intervals in
t\in\mathbb{R}
, for any
M\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
X\in\mathfrak{l}(\Lambda_{M},\mathbb{R})
.

Error estimates.

In this paragraph, we discuss some basic estimates of the approximation errors occurring in the proofs of Theorems
4.6
&
4.11
. Explicit control of the approximation errors is particularly interesting in view of the potential simulation of conformal field theories on quantum computers using a lattice discretization.

Let us begin with a discussion of the approximation error in Lemma
4.5
. The structure of the proof, and specifically considering,

\displaystyle\sum_{m\in\Gamma_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}}|f^{(N)}_{k}(M,m)-f_{k}(M,m)|^{2}

(204)

\displaystyle=\sum_{m\in\Gamma_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}}\left|\hat{s}(\varepsilon_{M}(m\!+\!k))(m\!+\!\tfrac{k}{2})\right|^{2}\left|\cos(\tfrac{1}{4}\varepsilon_{N}k)^{2}\tfrac{\sin(\varepsilon_{N}(m+\frac{k}{2}))}{\varepsilon_{N}(m+\frac{k}{2})}\tfrac{\hat{s}(\varepsilon_{N}m)}{\hat{s}(\varepsilon_{N}(m+k))}-1\right|^{2},

suggests a simple strategy to obtain a specific error bound by estimating against the decay of
\hat{s}
:

\displaystyle\sum_{m\in\Gamma_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}}\left|\hat{s}(\varepsilon_{M}(m\!+\!k))(m\!+\!\tfrac{k}{2})\right|^{2}\left|\cos(\tfrac{1}{4}\varepsilon_{N}k)^{2}\tfrac{\sin(\varepsilon_{N}(m+\frac{k}{2}))}{\varepsilon_{N}(m+\frac{k}{2})}\tfrac{\hat{s}(\varepsilon_{N}m)}{\hat{s}(\varepsilon_{N}(m+k))}-1\right|^{2}

(205)

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\left(\!\tfrac{2\pi^{2}}{L}\!\!\sup_{m\in\Gamma_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}}\!\!(1\!+\!\tfrac{L}{\pi}|m\!+\!\tfrac{k}{2}|)^{-2\delta}\!\left|\cos(\tfrac{1}{4}\varepsilon_{N}k)^{2}\tfrac{\sin(\varepsilon_{N}(m+\frac{k}{2}))}{\varepsilon_{N}(m+\frac{k}{2})}\tfrac{\hat{s}(\varepsilon_{N}m)}{\hat{s}(\varepsilon_{N}(m+k))}\!-\!1\right|^{2}\right)\|\hat{s}(\varepsilon_{M}(\!\ .\!\ \!+\!\tfrac{k}{2}))\|_{h^{1+\delta}}^{2},

for some
\delta>0
and
\|\hat{s}(\varepsilon_{M}(\!\ .\!\ +\tfrac{k}{2}))\|_{h^{1+\delta}}^{2}\!=\!\tfrac{1}{2L}\sum_{m\in\Gamma_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}}(1\!+\!\tfrac{L}{\pi}|m|)^{2(1+\delta)}|\hat{s}(\varepsilon_{M}(m\!+\!\tfrac{k}{2}))|^{2}
. The regularizing factor
(1\!+\!\tfrac{L}{\pi}|m\!+\!\tfrac{k}{2}|)^{-2\delta}
has the effect that,

\displaystyle\textup{Error}^{2}(\delta,L,k,N)

\displaystyle\!:=\!\tfrac{2\pi^{2}}{L}\!\!\sup_{m\in\Gamma_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}}\!\!\!\textup{error}^{2}(\delta,L,k,N,m)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle N\rightarrow\infty}}{{\longrightarrow}}0

(206)

\displaystyle \textup{error}^{2}(\delta,L,k,N,m)

\displaystyle\!:=\!(1+\tfrac{L}{\pi}|m+\tfrac{k}{2}|)^{-2\delta}\left|\cos(\tfrac{1}{4}\varepsilon_{N}k)^{2}\tfrac{\sin(\varepsilon_{N}(m+\frac{k}{2}))}{\varepsilon_{N}(m+\frac{k}{2})}\tfrac{\hat{s}(\varepsilon_{N}m)}{\hat{s}(\varepsilon_{N}(m+k))}\!-\!1\right|^{2},

with a rate determined by
\delta=\delta(L,k)
. To obtain a precise numerical bound we also need to estimate
\|\hat{s}(\varepsilon_{M}(\!\ .\!\ +\tfrac{k}{2}))\|_{h^{1+\delta}}^{2}
, which will decrease with increasing regularity of
s
.

According to the proof of Theorem
4.6
, we can estimate the Fock-space norm in (
192
) using (
204
):

\displaystyle\|(\alpha^{N}_{\infty}(L^{(N)}_{k,\pm})\!-\!L_{k,\pm})a^{\dagger}(\xi_{1})...a^{\dagger}(\xi_{n})\Omega_{0}\|

\displaystyle\!\!\leq\!\!\tfrac{nL}{\pi}\textup{Error}(\delta,L,k,N)\|\hat{s}(\varepsilon_{M}(\!\ .\!\ \!\!+\!\tfrac{k}{2}))\|_{h^{1+\delta}}\!\!\prod_{p=1}^{n}\!\|\eta_{p}\|_{M},

(207)

where
\xi_{p}=R^{M}_{\infty}(\eta_{p})
for
p=1,\dots,n
and
M<N
16
16

16

Note that the states
a^{\dagger}(\xi_{1})\dots a^{\dagger}(\xi_{n})\Omega_{0}
have the norm
\|a^{\dagger}(\xi_{1})\dots a^{\dagger}(\xi_{n})\Omega_{0}\|=\prod_{p=1}^{n}\|\xi_{p}\|_{\infty}
, for
\xi_{1},\dots,\xi_{n}
orthogonal.
.

The supremum in (
206
) can be estimated for
\delta\in[0,2]
as follows:

\displaystyle\textup{Error}^{2}(\delta,L,k,N)

(208)

\displaystyle=\tfrac{2\pi^{2}}{L}(\tfrac{\pi}{L_{N}})^{2\delta}\!\!\sup_{m\in\Gamma_{\infty}}\!\!(\tfrac{\pi}{L_{N}}\!+\!\varepsilon_{N}|m+\tfrac{k}{2}|)^{-2\delta}\!\left|\cos(\tfrac{1}{4}\varepsilon_{N}k)^{2}\tfrac{\sin(\varepsilon_{N}(m+\frac{k}{2}))}{\varepsilon_{N}(m+\frac{k}{2})}\tfrac{\hat{s}(\varepsilon_{N}m)}{\hat{s}(\varepsilon_{N}(m+k))}-1\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{2\pi^{2}}{L}(\tfrac{\pi}{L_{N}})^{2\delta}\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}(\tfrac{\pi}{L_{N}}\!+\!|x+\varepsilon_{N}\tfrac{k}{2}|)^{-2\delta}\!\left|\cos(\tfrac{1}{4}\varepsilon_{N}k)^{2}\tfrac{\sin(x+\varepsilon_{N}\frac{k}{2})}{x+\varepsilon_{N}\frac{k}{2}}\tfrac{\hat{s}(x)}{\hat{s}(x+\varepsilon_{N}k)}-1\right|^{2}.

Thus, we find for
k=0
:

\displaystyle\textup{Error}^{2}(\delta,L,0,N)

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{2\pi^{2}}{L}(\tfrac{\pi}{L_{N}})^{2\delta}\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}(\tfrac{\pi}{L_{N}}\!+\!|x|)^{-2\delta}\!\left|\tfrac{\sin(x)}{x}-1\right|^{2}

(209)

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{2\pi^{2}}{L}(\tfrac{\pi}{L_{N}})^{2\delta}\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}|x|^{-2\delta}\!\left|\tfrac{\sin(x)}{x}-1\right|^{2},

and
\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}|x|^{-2\delta}\!\left|\tfrac{\sin(x)}{x}-1\right|^{2}
is a finite constant of order unity for
\delta\in[0,2]
since
|\tfrac{\sin(x)}{x}-1|^{2}\approx\tfrac{x^{4}}{36}+\mathcal{O}(x^{6})
. As long as
N
is sufficiently large, we expect essentially the same behavior for
k\neq 0
because of the uniform continuity of
\hat{s}
in (
208
). This suggests:

\displaystyle\textup{Error}^{2}(\delta,L,k,N)

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{\delta,L,k}\!\ 2^{-2\delta N},

(210)

with an explicitly computable proportionality factor
C_{\delta,L,k}
.

Remark 4.20

(CFT simulation)
.

Concerning the simulation of the a free-fermion CFT on a quantum computer, the scale parameter
N
reflects the number of qubits used in the simulation because it determines the size of the real-space lattice
\Lambda_{N}
which forms the basis for the Hilbert space on which the KS approximant
L^{(N)}_{\pm,k}
acts. The second scale parameter
M
reflects the resolution at which the Fock states
a^{\dagger}(\xi_{1})\dots a^{\dagger}(\xi_{n})\Omega_{0}
are resolved. It should, therefore, be strictly less than
N
reflecting at least a doubling of the sampling frequency in accordance with the Nyquist-Shannon theorem. At the same time, if we fix the length scale
L
of the spatial circle
S^{1}_{L}
on which the CFT is supposed to live, we are forced to choose
M
in accordance with the regularity parameter
K\geq 2
of the Daubechies scaling function
s=\!\ _{K}s
since
\supp(\!\ _{K}s)=[0,2K-1]
. This suggest a lower bound on
M
in terms of
K
according to
\varepsilon_{M}(2K-1)\sim L
to ensure that
s^{(\varepsilon_{M})}
is well-localized in
S^{1}_{L}
.

The choice,
\xi_{p}=R^{M}_{\infty}(\eta_{p})
,
p=1,\dots,n
, for the one-particle states means that an arbitrary
n
-particle state
a^{\dagger}(\xi_{1})\dots a^{\dagger}(\xi_{n})\Omega_{0}
is approximated by projecting its one-particle amplitudes onto the subspace generated by the wavelet basis determined by the scaling function
s
up to a resolution scale
\varepsilon_{M}=2^{-M}\varepsilon_{0}
, i.e.:

\displaystyle\eta_{p}

\displaystyle=R^{M}_{\infty}{}^{*}\xi_{p}=\sum_{x\in\Lambda_{M}}\langle s^{(\varepsilon_{M})}(\!\ .\!\ -x),\xi_{p}\rangle s^{(\varepsilon_{M})}(\!\ .\!\ -x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{x\in\Lambda_{0}}\langle s(\!\ .\!\ -x),\xi_{p}\rangle s(\!\ .\!\ -x)+\sum_{j=0}^{M}\sum_{x\in\Lambda_{j}}\langle\psi^{(\varepsilon_{j})}(\!\ .\!\ -x),\xi_{p}\rangle\psi^{(\varepsilon_{j})}(\!\ .\!\ -x),

where
\psi
is the wavelet associated with
s
.

The inner products of the states
\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}(a^{\dagger}(\xi_{1}))...\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}(a^{\dagger}(\xi_{n}))\Omega_{0}
are accessible according to Lemma
3.11
in terms of the lattice correlation functions of
\omega^{(N)}_{0,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}
:

\displaystyle\langle\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}(a^{\dagger}(R^{M}_{\infty}(\eta)))\Omega_{0},\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}(a^{\dagger}(R^{M}_{\infty}(\eta^{\prime})))\Omega_{0}\rangle

\displaystyle=\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\langle a^{\dagger}_{N}(R^{M}_{N}(\eta))\omega^{(N)}_{0,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}},a^{\dagger}_{N}(R^{M}_{N}(\eta^{\prime}))\omega^{(N)}_{0,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}\rangle,

where we explicitly indicated that the creation operators
a^{\dagger}_{N}
are to be realized by the quantum simulation at scale
N
.

Let us continue with a few remarks on the estimation of errors occurring in Lemma
4.10
using the momentum-cutoff renormalization group.

Remark 4.21

(Error of time-evolution)
.

The error bound on the one-particle generators directly translates to an error bound on the unitary implementer of time evolution (
k=0
) by the fundamental theorem of calculus since
[\tilde{\ell}^{(N)}_{\pm,0},\exp(it\ell_{\pm,0})]=0
implies:

\displaystyle\|(e^{it\tilde{\ell}^{(N)}_{\pm,0}}-e^{it\ell_{\pm,0}})R^{M}_{\infty}(\eta_{p})\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq t\|(\tilde{\ell}^{(N)}_{\pm,0}-\ell_{\pm,0})R^{M}_{\infty}(\eta_{p})\|_{\infty}.

(211)

Thus, we can explicitly estimate the error for the analogue of (
195
) as well as the approximation of time-dependent correlation functions as in Theorem
6.1
below. For the same reason, we can estimate the approximation of correlation functions involving
U_{t}=\exp(itL_{\pm,0})
by those involving
U^{(N)}_{t}=\exp(itdF_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}}(\tilde{\ell}^{(N)}_{\pm,0}))
, cf. Corollary
4.12
. By a similar reasoning that leads to (
210
), we have for
\delta\in[0,2]
:

\displaystyle\|((\tilde{\ell}^{(N)}_{\pm,0}\!-\!\ell_{\pm,0})^{(t)}_{\pm\pm}R^{M}_{\infty}(\eta_{p})\|_{\infty}^{2}

\displaystyle\!\leq\!\varepsilon_{N}^{2\delta}\underbrace{(\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}\tfrac{|\sinc(x)\!-\!1|^{2}}{|x|^{2\delta}})}_{=\frac{1}{36}\textup{ for }\delta=2}\|\eta\|_{M}^{2}(\tfrac{L}{\pi})^{2}\!\!\!\!\sum_{n\in\Gamma_{M,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}}\!\!\!\!\theta(\pm n)|n|^{2(1+\delta)},

(212)

which shows that the asymptotic approximation error for the time-evolution behaves as
\sim 2^{-2N}
for fixed
M
(
\delta=2
).

Remark 4.22

(Error estimates for
k\neq 0
)
.

For
k\neq 0
, the error bound on the generators can be used to obtain an error bound (asymptotic in the time
t
) for the associated unitary groups acting on analytic vectors
\{e_{m}\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
which are in the image of
R^{M}_{\infty}
. To this end, we observe that:

\displaystyle\|(e^{it\tilde{o}^{(N)}}-e^{ito})\xi\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq t \|(\tilde{o}^{(N)}-o)\xi\|_{\infty}+\sum_{j=2}^{\infty}\tfrac{t^{j}}{j!}\|((\tilde{o}^{(N)})^{j}-o^{j})\xi\|_{\infty},

which is convergent for some
t>0
whenever
\xi
is analytic for
\tilde{o}^{(N)}
,
o
. Thus, we can, for example, consider
\tilde{o}^{(N)}=\tilde{r}^{(N)}_{\pm,k}
and
o=r_{\pm,k}
and exploit (
187
) to deduce for
k\neq 0
:

\displaystyle\|((\tilde{r}^{(N)}_{\pm,k})^{j}-r_{\pm,k}^{j})e_{m}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq 4L(\tfrac{L}{2\pi})^{j}\binom{2j}{j}^{\frac{1}{2}}|k|^{j}\tfrac{\Gamma(|\frac{m}{k}|+\frac{1}{2}+j)}{\Gamma(|\frac{m}{k}|+\frac{1}{2})}.

This immediately implies (for sufficiently small
t>0
if
k\neq 0
):

\displaystyle\|(e^{it\tilde{r}^{(N)}_{\pm,k}}-e^{itr_{\pm,k}})e_{m}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq t\|(\tilde{r}^{(N)}_{\pm,k}-r_{\pm,k})e_{m}\|_{\infty}+4L\frac{(\tfrac{L}{\pi}(|m|+\frac{1}{2}|k|)t)^{2}}{1-\tfrac{L}{\pi}(|m|+\frac{1}{2}|k|)t},

(213)

because
\binom{2j}{j}^{\frac{1}{2}}|k|^{j}\tfrac{\Gamma(|\frac{m}{k}|+\frac{1}{2}+j)}{\Gamma(|\frac{m}{k}|+\frac{1}{2})}\leq(2(|m|+\tfrac{1}{2}|k|))^{j}j!
. An analogous bound holds for the approximation of the unitary group associated with
\iota_{\pm,k}
.

Thus, we find that for small
t>0
(independent of
N
) the approximation error (in the limit
N\rightarrow\infty
) for the unitary groups can be explicitly estimated in terms of the error given for the generators.

Being less explicit about the one-particle vectors used in (
201
) (or (
195
) in general), we can always get the following bound for the approximation error, valid for all
t>0
, when restricting to a spectral subspace
\mathfrak{h}_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}^{\leq M}=P_{[M,-M]}\mathfrak{h}^{(\pm)}_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}
of
o
for some finite cut-off
M<\infty
,

\displaystyle\|(e^{it\tilde{o}^{(N)}}\!-\!e^{ito})\xi\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\!\leq\!t \|(\tilde{o}^{(N)}\!-\!o)\xi\|_{\infty}\!+\!(1\!+\!a)(e^{Mt}\!-\!(1\!+\!Mt))\|\xi\|_{\infty}\!+\!b(e^{t}\!-\!(1\!+\!t))\|\xi\|_{\infty},

with
\xi\in\mathfrak{h}_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}^{\leq M}
, and assuming the powers of the approximants
(\tilde{o}^{(N)})^{j}
are constructed as uniformly
o^{j}
-bounded operators, i.e.
\|(\tilde{o}^{(N)})^{j}\xi\|_{\infty}\leq a\|o^{j}\xi\|_{\infty}+b\|\xi\|_{\infty}
.

4.2.5
Koo-Saleur approximants of the equivalent XY model

For completeness and in view of our companion article
[
72
]
, we also provide the expressions for the KS approximants in terms of the single-component fermion
b
using (
79
):

\displaystyle L_{k}^{(N)}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{8\pi}\sum_{l,l’\in\Gamma_{N+1,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}}\!\!\!\begin{pmatrix}\hat{b}_{l’}\\
\hat{b}^{{\dagger}}_{-l’}\end{pmatrix}^{{\dagger}}e^{-\frac{i}{4}\varepsilon_{N}k}\delta_{k,l’-l\!\!\!\!\mod\frac{2\pi}{\varepsilon_{N}}}\ell^{(N)}_{k}(l’,l)\begin{pmatrix}\hat{b}_{l}\\
\hat{b}^{{\dagger}}_{-l}\end{pmatrix},

(214)

\displaystyle\ell^{(N)}_{k}(l’,l)

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}\begin{pmatrix}\hskip-56.9055pt-e^{\frac{i}{4}\varepsilon_{N}k}\sin(\varepsilon_{N}(l+\tfrac{k}{2}))&\hskip-56.9055pt-i(e^{\frac{i}{4}\varepsilon_{N}k}\sin(\varepsilon_{N}\tfrac{l}{2})+e^{-\frac{i}{4}\varepsilon_{N}k}\sin(\varepsilon_{N}\tfrac{l+k}{2}))\\[2.84544pt]
i(e^{\frac{i}{4}\varepsilon_{N}k}\sin(\varepsilon_{N}\tfrac{l’-k}{2})+e^{-\frac{i}{4}\varepsilon_{N}k}\sin(\varepsilon_{N}\tfrac{l’}{2}))&-e^{-\frac{i}{4}\varepsilon_{N}k}\sin(\varepsilon_{N}\tfrac{l+l’}{2})\end{pmatrix},

\displaystyle\overline{L}_{k}^{(N)}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{8\pi}\sum_{l,l’\in\Gamma_{N+1,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}}\!\!\!\begin{pmatrix}\hat{b}_{l’}\\
\hat{b}^{{\dagger}}_{-l’}\end{pmatrix}^{{\dagger}}e^{\frac{i}{4}\varepsilon_{N}k}\delta_{-k,l’-l\!\!\!\!\mod\frac{2\pi}{\varepsilon_{N}}}\overline{\ell}^{(N)}_{k}(l’,l)\begin{pmatrix}\hat{b}_{l}\\
\hat{b}^{{\dagger}}_{-l}\end{pmatrix},

\displaystyle\overline{\ell}^{(N)}_{k}(l’,l)

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}\begin{pmatrix}\hskip-56.9055pte^{-\frac{i}{4}\varepsilon_{N}k}\sin(\varepsilon_{N}(l-\tfrac{k}{2}))&\hskip-56.9055pt-i(e^{-\frac{i}{4}\varepsilon_{N}k}\sin(\varepsilon_{N}\tfrac{l}{2})+e^{\frac{i}{4}\varepsilon_{N}k}\sin(\varepsilon_{N}\tfrac{l-k}{2}))\\[2.84544pt]
i(e^{-\frac{i}{4}\varepsilon_{N}k}\sin(\varepsilon_{N}\tfrac{l’+k}{2})+e^{\frac{i}{4}\varepsilon_{N}k}\sin(\varepsilon_{N}\tfrac{l’}{2}))&e^{\frac{i}{4}\varepsilon_{N}k}\sin(\varepsilon_{N}\tfrac{l+l’}{2})\end{pmatrix}.

Alternatively, we may derive the KS approximants directly in terms of the single-component fermion on the doubled lattice
\Lambda_{N+1}
, which results in:

\displaystyle L_{k}^{(N)}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{4\pi}\sum_{l,l’\in\Gamma_{N+1}}\!\!\!\begin{pmatrix}\hat{b}_{l’}\\
\hat{b}^{{\dagger}}_{-l’}\end{pmatrix}^{{\dagger}}\ell^{(N)}_{k}(l’,l)\begin{pmatrix}\hat{b}_{l}\\
\hat{b}^{{\dagger}}_{-l}\end{pmatrix},

(215)

\displaystyle\ell^{(N)}_{k}(l’,l)

\displaystyle=\delta_{k,l’-l}\begin{pmatrix}-\sin(\varepsilon_{N+1}(l+l’))&e^{-i\varepsilon_{N+1}(l+\frac{k}{2})}\cos(\tfrac{1}{2}\varepsilon_{N+1}k)\\
e^{i\varepsilon_{N+1}(l’-\frac{k}{2})}\cos(\tfrac{1}{2}\varepsilon_{N+1}k)&-\sin(\varepsilon_{N+1}(l+l’))\end{pmatrix},

\displaystyle\overline{L}_{k}^{(N)}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{4\pi}\sum_{l,l’\in\Gamma_{N+1}}\!\!\!\begin{pmatrix}\hat{b}_{l’}\\
\hat{b}^{{\dagger}}_{-l’}\end{pmatrix}^{{\dagger}}\overline{\ell}^{(N)}_{k}(l’,l)\begin{pmatrix}\hat{b}_{l}\\
\hat{b}^{{\dagger}}_{-l}\end{pmatrix},

\displaystyle\overline{\ell}^{(N)}_{k}(l’,l)

\displaystyle=\delta_{-k,l’-l}\begin{pmatrix}\sin(\varepsilon_{N+1}(l+l’))&e^{-i\varepsilon_{N+1}(l-\frac{k}{2})}\cos(\tfrac{1}{2}\varepsilon_{N+1}k)\\
e^{i\varepsilon_{N+1}(l’+\frac{k}{2})}\cos(\tfrac{1}{2}\varepsilon_{N+1}k)&\sin(\varepsilon_{N+1}(l+l’))\end{pmatrix}.

But, it should be noted that these alternative expressions are not directly comparable to those in (
214
) because they arise through the Fourier transform on
\Lambda_{N+1}
, not
\Lambda_{N}
, and, therefore, refer to the (symmetrized) Hamiltonian density

\displaystyle\tilde{h}^{(N)}_{x}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}\varepsilon_{N+1}^{-2}(b_{x}b_{x+\varepsilon_{N+1}}-b^{{\dagger}}_{x}b^{{\dagger}}_{x+\varepsilon_{N+1}}+b_{x-\varepsilon_{N+1}}b_{x}-b^{{\dagger}}_{x-\varepsilon_{N+1}}b^{{\dagger}}_{x}),

of the single-component fermion which is related to the Hamiltonian density of the two-component fermion by the half-shift
x\mapsto x+\varepsilon_{N+1}
:

\displaystyle h^{(N)}_{x}

\displaystyle=\tilde{h}^{(N)}_{x}+\tilde{h}^{(N)}_{x+\varepsilon_{N+1}},

\displaystyle x

\displaystyle\in\Lambda_{N}.

(216)

5
Approximation of Wess-Zumino-Witten currents

We use the same approach that shows the convergence of the KS approximants to the Virasoro generators in the scaling limit to approximate the currents of some Wess-Zumino-Witten models. Similar to the Virasoro generators and the KS approximants the currents of these WZW models can be obtained as normal-ordered second quantizations of certain one-particle operators
[
21
,
26
,
91
]
, i.e. normal-ordered fermion bilinears.

The
U(1)
-current.

For simplicity, we describe the adaptation of the procedure for the (chiral)
U(1)
-current,

\displaystyle J_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}}(x)

\displaystyle=:\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}}(\psi_{+|x}^{{\dagger}})\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}|x}(\psi_{\pm}):,

\displaystyle x

\displaystyle\in S^{1}_{L},

(217)

in the Neveu-Schwarz sector of
\mathfrak{A}^{(+)}_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}}
first.
J_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}}(x)
is a local bosonic field on
\mathfrak{F}_{\textup{a}}(\mathfrak{h}^{(+)}_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}})
with the following commutation relations
[
75
]
:

\displaystyle[J_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}}(x),J_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}}(y)]=\tfrac{i}{2\pi}\delta^{\prime}_{0}(x-y)\;,

(218)

which reflect the presence of a non-trivial central charge
c=1
. At finite scales, the direct analogue of
J_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}}(x)
is given by the
lattice current
:

\displaystyle J^{(N)}(x)

\displaystyle=\psi_{+|x}^{{\dagger}}\psi_{+|x},

\displaystyle x

\displaystyle\in\Lambda_{N},

(219)

as an element of
\mathfrak{A}^{(+)}_{N,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}}
. As for the Virasoro generators,
J_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}}
can be approximated by
J^{(N)}
in the scaling limit representation
\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}}
on
\mathfrak{F}_{\textup{a}}(\mathfrak{h}^{(+)}_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}})
using the asymptotic maps
\alpha^{N}_{\infty}:\mathfrak{A}^{(+)}_{N,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}}\rightarrow\mathfrak{A}^{(+)}_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}}
as in the Theorem
4.6
using the wavelet renormalization group,

\displaystyle:\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}}(\alpha^{N}_{\infty}(J^{(N)}(x)\!)\!):\!\

\displaystyle=\ :\!\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}}(\psi_{+}^{{\dagger}}(s^{(\varepsilon_{N})}_{x})\!)\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}}(\psi_{+}(s^{(\varepsilon_{N})}_{x})\!):\;,

(220)

and similar for the momentum-cutoff renormalization group as in Theorems
4.11
and
4.16
.

We infer from (
220
) that the approximants for the wavelet renormalization group are localized operators in the even subalgebra of
\mathfrak{A}^{(+)}_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}}
with a localization region determined by the support of the scaling function
s^{(\varepsilon_{N})}_{x}
.

In momentum space, we have the following picture:

\displaystyle\hat{J}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-},k}

\displaystyle=\ :\!dF_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}}(j_{k})\!:\ =\tfrac{1}{2L}\!\!\!\sum_{l\in\Gamma_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}}}\!\!\!:\!\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}}(\hat{\psi}^{{\dagger}}_{+|l+k})\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}}(\hat{\psi}_{+|l})\!:,

(221)

\displaystyle\hat{J}^{(N)}_{k}

\displaystyle=dF_{0}(j^{(N)}_{k})=\tfrac{1}{2L_{N}}\!\!\!\sum_{l\in\Gamma_{N,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}}}\!\hat{\psi}^{{\dagger}}_{+|l+k}\hat{\psi}_{+|l},

such that
:\!\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}}(\alpha^{N}_{\infty}(J^{(N)}_{k}))\!:\ =\ :\!dF_{C}(\tilde{j}^{(N)}_{k})\!:
with
\tilde{j}^{(N)}_{k}=R^{N}_{\infty}j^{(N)}_{k}(R^{N}_{\infty})^{*}
.
The one-particle operators
j^{(N)}_{k}
,
j_{k}
are the (unitary) momentum-space translations on
\mathfrak{h}^{(+)}_{N,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}}
respectively
\mathfrak{h}^{(+)}_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}}
:

\displaystyle(j^{(N)}_{k}\hat{\xi})_{m}

\displaystyle=\hat{\xi}_{m-k},

\displaystyle(j_{k}\hat{\xi})_{m}

\displaystyle=\hat{\xi}_{m-k},

(222)

for
\xi\in\mathfrak{h}^{(+)}_{N,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}}
or
\mathfrak{h}^{(+)}_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}}
, satisfying the implementability condition (
19
) and with matrix elements:

\displaystyle(j^{(N)}_{k})_{mn}

\displaystyle=2L_{N}\delta^{(N)}_{m,n+k},

\displaystyle(j_{k})_{mn}

\displaystyle=2L\delta_{m,n+k}.

(223)

Since the off-diagonal parts of
j_{k}
satisfy the Hilbert-Schmidt condition,

\displaystyle\|(j_{k})_{\pm\mp}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{n\in\Gamma_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}}}\theta(\pm n)\theta(\mp(n-k))<\infty,

(224)

and,

\displaystyle j^{(N)}_{k}{}^{*}

\displaystyle=j^{(N)}_{-k},

\displaystyle j_{k}^{*}

\displaystyle=j_{k},

(225)

the expression for
\hat{J}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-},k}
in (
221
) results in a densely defined, closable operator on
\mathfrak{F}_{\textup{a}}(\mathfrak{h}^{(+)}_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}})

[
14
,
26
]
. As before, we define the smeared
U(1)
- and lattice current,

\displaystyle J^{(N)}(X)

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2L_{N}}\sum_{k\in\Gamma_{N,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}+}}}\hat{X}_{k}J^{(N)}_{k},

\displaystyle J_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}}(X)

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2L}\sum_{k\in\Gamma_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}+}}}\hat{X}_{k}\hat{J}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-},k},

(226)

and their one-particle analogues,

\displaystyle j^{(N)}(X)

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2L_{N}}\sum_{k\in\Gamma_{N,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}+}}}\hat{X}_{k}j^{(N)}_{k},

\displaystyle j(X)

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2L}\sum_{k\in\Gamma_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}+}}}\hat{X}_{k}j_{k},

(227)

for with sufficiently regular
X\in\mathfrak{l}^{\alpha}(S^{1}_{L},\mathbb{C})
respectively
X\in\mathfrak{l}(\Lambda_{N},\mathbb{C}):
. For real
X=\overline{X}
, i.e.
iX\in\mathfrak{u}(1)
, the Lie algebra of
U(1)
,
J_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}}(X)
and
J^{(N)}(X)
are self-adjoint and induce automorphic Bogoliubov transformations of
\mathfrak{A}^{(+)}_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}}
respectively
\mathfrak{A}^{(+)}_{N,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}}
:

\displaystyle\Ad_{e^{itJ^{(N)}(X)}}(a^{\dagger}(\xi))

\displaystyle=a^{\dagger}(e^{itj^{(N)}(X)}\xi),

\displaystyle\Ad_{e^{itJ_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}}(X)}}(\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}}(a^{\dagger}(\xi)))

\displaystyle=\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}}(a^{\dagger}(e^{itj(X)}\xi)),

(228)

for
\xi\in\mathfrak{h}^{(+)}_{N,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}}
or
\mathfrak{h}^{(+)}_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}}
, and
t\in\mathbb{R}
.

Comparing the matrix elements (
223
) with those of the one-particle operators of the Virasoro generators and the KS approximants (
199
), we deduce that all convergence results of Section
4.2.4
equally apply to the approximation of the
U(1)
-current by (
219
) in the scaling limit. Notably, some of the proof of the results for the
U(1)
-current simplify slightly because the one-particle operators
j_{k}
,
k\in\Gamma_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}+}}
, are bounded. As an illustration we state the analogue of Theorem
4.16
:

Theorem 5.1

(Convergence of smeared lattice currents for
\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}}
)
.

Let
s\in C^{\alpha}(\mathbb{R})
be a sufficiently regular, compactly supported orthonormal Daubechies scaling function, and let
X\in\mathfrak{l}(\Lambda_{M},\mathbb{C})
for some
M\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Then, the smeared lattice currents,
J^{(N)}(S^{M}_{N}(X))
, converge strongly to the
U(1)
-current,
J_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}}(S^{M}_{\infty}(X))
, on the dense domain
\mathfrak{F}^{\alg}_{\textup{a}}(\mathcal{D}_{\std})\subset\mathfrak{F}_{\textup{a}}(\mathfrak{h}^{(\pm)}_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}})
spanned by anti-symmetric Fock vectors with finitely many one-particle excitations in
\mathcal{D}_{\std}
(finite
\mathcal{D}_{\std}
-particle number):

\displaystyle\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\|(:\!\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}}(\alpha^{N}_{\infty}(J^{(N)}(S^{M}_{N}(X)))\!)\!:-J_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}}(S^{M}_{\infty}(X)))a^{\dagger}(\xi_{1})\dots a^{\dagger}(\xi_{n})\Omega_{0}\|

\displaystyle=0,

(229)

for all
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
\xi_{1},\dots,\xi_{n}\in\mathcal{D}_{\std}
.

Proof.

The proof is identical to that Theorem
4.16
using the analogue of Lemma
4.15
with the additional simplification that we have uniform bounds:

\displaystyle\|\tilde{j}^{(N)}_{k}R^{K}_{\infty}(e_{l})\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=1,

\displaystyle\|j_{k}R^{K}_{\infty}(e_{l})\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=1.

\Box

WZW-currents.

We illustrate the adaptation of the procedure for the
U(1)
-current to non-abelian currents by the example of the level-
1
(chiral)
\hat{\mathfrak{u}}(D)_{1}
-currents. Here,
\mathfrak{u}(D)
denotes the Lie algebra of
U(D)
, the unitary group in
D
dimensions. The
\hat{\mathfrak{u}}(D)_{1}
-currents are given by:

\displaystyle J^{\mu}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}}(x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j=1}^{D}t^{\mu}_{ij}:\!\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}}(\psi^{(i){\dagger}}_{+|x})\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}}(\psi^{(j)}_{+|x})\!:\ =\tfrac{1}{2L}\sum_{k\in\Gamma_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}}}e^{-ikx}\hat{J}^{\mu}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-},k}\;,

(230)

where the matrices
\{t^{\mu}\}_{\mu=1}^{D^{2}-1}
are a basis of the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{u}(D)
, and we use
D
-component (chiral) complex fermions
\mathfrak{A}^{(+)}_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}}(D)=\mathfrak{A}_{\CAR}(\mathfrak{h}^{(+)}_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{D})
. The finite-scale analogues of the non-abelian currents and the associated approximants are given by:

\displaystyle J^{(N),\mu}(x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j=1}^{D}t^{\mu}_{ij}\psi^{(i){\dagger}}_{+|x}\psi^{(j)}_{+|x}=\tfrac{1}{2L_{N}}\!\!\!\sum_{k\in\Gamma_{N,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}}}e^{-ikx}\hat{J}^{(N),\mu}_{k},

\displaystyle\hat{J}^{(N),\mu}_{k}

\displaystyle=dF_{0}(j^{(N),\mu}_{k}),

(231)

with the one-particle operators that also satisfy the implementability condition (
19
):

\displaystyle(j^{(N),\mu}_{k}\hat{\xi})_{i,m}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{D}t^{\mu}_{ij}\hat{\xi}_{j,m-k},

\displaystyle(j^{\mu}_{k}\hat{\xi})_{i,m}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{D}t^{\mu}_{ij}\hat{\xi}_{j,m-k},

(232)

for
\mathfrak{h}^{(+)}_{N,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{D}
respectively
\xi\in\mathfrak{h}^{(+)}_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{D}
. The finite-scale one-particle operators are mapped to the scaling limit by
\tilde{j}^{(N),\mu}_{k}=R^{N}_{\infty}(j^{(N),\mu}_{k})(R^{N}_{\infty})^{*}
. where asymptotic maps are used componentwise.

It is natural to use the Hilbert-Schmidt norm
\|\!\ .\!\ \|_{2,D}
on the basis elements
t^{\mu}
as it is directly related to the Killing form on
\mathfrak{u}(D)
, and by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality we have:

\displaystyle\|j^{(N),\mu}_{k}\hat{\eta}\|_{\mathfrak{h}^{(+)}_{N,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{D}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|t^{\mu}\|_{2,D}^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\|(j^{(N)}_{k}\hat{\eta})_{i}\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{N}}^{2}=\|t^{\mu}\|_{2,D}^{2}\|\eta\|_{\mathfrak{h}^{(+)}_{N,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{D}}^{2},

(233)

\displaystyle\|j^{\mu}_{k}\hat{\xi}\|_{\mathfrak{h}^{(+)}_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{D}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|t^{\mu}\|_{2,D}^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\|(j_{k}\hat{\xi})_{i}\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{\infty}}^{2}=\|t^{\mu}\|_{2,D}^{2}\|\xi\|_{\mathfrak{h}^{(+)}_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{D}}^{2}.

The convergence results for the non-abelian lattice currents as, for example, in Theorem
5.1
follow directly from the estimate:

\displaystyle\|(\tilde{j}^{(N),\mu}_{k}-j^{\mu}_{k})R^{K}_{\infty}(\eta)\|_{\mathfrak{h}^{(+)}_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{D}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|t^{\mu}\|_{2,D}^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\|(\tilde{j}^{(N)}_{k}-j_{k})R^{K}_{\infty}(\eta_{i})\|_{\infty}^{2},

(234)

as this reduce the problem to setting of the
U(1)
-current.

A similar reasoning applies to any non-abelian
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}_{k}
-current of the form (
230
) associated with a (simply-laced) Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
and representation
\lambda
determining the level
k=k(\lambda)
, possibly by using the Majorana algebras
\mathfrak{B}^{(+)}_{N,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}}
and
\mathfrak{B}^{(+)}_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}}
instead.

6
Approximation of correlation functions

The convergence results of the previous sections for the approximation of Virasoro generator, their unitaries and the associated Bogoliubov transformations allow for the approximation of (chiral) correlation functions of the resulting fermionic continuum field theories in the scaling limit. Explicit error bounds can be obtained along the lines outlined in the last paragraph of Section
4.2.4
.

Fermion correlation function.

Using either the wavelet or the momentum-cutoff renormalization group, we obtain the following approximation theorem concerning the dynamical (chiral) correlation functions of fermions. We state the theorem for complex fermions
\mathfrak{A}^{(\pm)}_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}
, but an analogous statement holds for the Majorana fermions
\mathfrak{B}^{(\pm)}_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}
as is clear from the structure of the proofs in Section
4.2.4
.

Theorem 6.1

(Convergence of fermion correlation functions)
.

Given the quasi-free representations
\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}
of the fermion algebra
\mathfrak{A}^{(\pm)}_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}
arising from the scaling limit states
\omega_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}
. Then, for any
M\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
A,B\in\mathfrak{A}^{(\pm)}_{M,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}
and uniformly in
t\in\mathbb{R}
on compact intervals, we have:

\displaystyle\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}(\omega^{(N)}_{0,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}},\pi^{(N)}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}(\alpha^{M}_{N}(A))\sigma^{(N)}_{t}(\pi^{(N)}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}(\alpha^{M}_{N}(B)))\omega^{(N)}_{0,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}})

\displaystyle=(\Omega_{0},\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}(\alpha^{M}_{\infty}(A))\sigma_{t}(\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}(\alpha^{M}_{\infty}(B)))\Omega_{0})\;,

where
\omega^{(N)}_{0,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}
,
\pi^{(N)}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}
are the GNS-vector and -representation of
\omega^{(N)}_{0,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}
, and
\Omega_{0}\in\mathfrak{F}_{\textup{a}}(\mathfrak{h}^{(\pm)}_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}})
is the standard Fock vacuum.
\sigma^{(N)}_{t}
and
\sigma_{t}
are
1
-parameter (semi-)groups of (automorphic) Bogoliubov transformations generated by a finite-scale KS approximant (
145
) or a (non-)abelian current (
219
) & (
231
) and their scaling limits respectively.

Proof.

Let us first introduce the short hands:

\displaystyle C^{(N)}_{t}(A,B)

\displaystyle=(\omega^{(N)}_{0,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}},\pi^{(N)}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}(A)\sigma^{(N)}_{t}(\pi^{(N)}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}(B))\omega^{(N)}_{0,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}),

\displaystyle C_{t}(A,B)

\displaystyle=(\Omega_{0},\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}(A)\sigma_{t}(\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}(B))\Omega_{0})\;,

for either
A,B\in\mathfrak{A}^{(\pm)}_{N,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}
or
A,B\in\mathfrak{A}^{(\pm)}_{\infty,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}
, as well as,

\displaystyle\tilde{C}^{(N)}_{t}(A,B)

\displaystyle=(\Omega_{0},\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}(A)\tilde{\sigma}^{(N)}_{t}(\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}(B))\Omega_{0})\;,

with the approximation
\tilde{\sigma}^{(N)}_{t}
of
\sigma_{t}
given by
\tilde{\sigma}^{(N)}_{t}=\Ad_{\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}(\alpha^{N}_{\infty}(U_{N}(t)))}
because
\sigma^{(N)}_{t}=\Ad_{U_{N}(t)}
is inner by construction, i.e.
U_{N}(t)\in\mathfrak{A}^{(\pm)}_{N,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}
. Thus, the statement can be rephrased as:

\displaystyle\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}C^{(N)}_{t}(\alpha^{M}_{N}(A),\alpha^{M}_{N}(B))

\displaystyle=C_{t}(\alpha^{M}_{\infty}(A),\alpha^{M}_{\infty}(B)).

We observe that:

\displaystyle C^{(N)}_{t}(\alpha^{M}_{N}(A),\alpha^{M}_{N}(B))

\displaystyle=(\omega^{(N)}_{0,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}},\pi^{(N)}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}(\alpha^{M}_{N}(A)\Ad_{U_{N}(t)}(\alpha^{M}_{N}(B)))\omega^{(N)}_{0,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}})

(235)

\displaystyle=\omega^{(N)}_{0,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}(\alpha^{M}_{N}(A)\Ad_{U_{N}(t)}(\alpha^{M}_{N}(B))),

\displaystyle\tilde{C}^{(N)}_{t}(\alpha^{M}_{\infty}(A),\alpha^{M}_{\infty}(B))

\displaystyle=(\Omega_{0},(\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}\circ\alpha^{N}_{\infty})(\alpha^{M}_{N}(A)\Ad_{U_{N}(t)}(\alpha^{M}_{N}(B)))\Omega_{0})

\displaystyle=\omega_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}(\alpha^{N}_{\infty}(\alpha^{M}_{N}(A)\Ad_{U_{N}(t)}(\alpha^{M}_{N}(B))))\;,

Thus, we can estimate:

\displaystyle|C^{(N)}_{t}\!(\alpha^{M}_{N}\!(A),\alpha^{M}_{N}\!(B)\!)\!-\!C_{t}(\alpha^{M}_{\infty}\!(A),\alpha^{M}_{\infty}\!(B)\!)|

\displaystyle\!\leq\!|C^{(N)}_{t}\!(\alpha^{M}_{N}\!(A),\alpha^{M}_{N}\!(B)\!)\!-\!\tilde{C}^{(N)}_{t}\!(\alpha^{M}_{\infty}\!(A),\alpha^{M}_{\infty}\!(B)\!)|\!+\!|\tilde{C}^{(N)}_{t}\!(\alpha^{M}_{\infty}\!(A),\alpha^{M}_{\infty}\!(B)\!)\!-\!C_{t}(\alpha^{M}_{\infty}\!(A),\alpha^{M}_{\infty}\!(B)\!)|

\displaystyle\!\leq\!|(\Omega_{0},\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}(\alpha^{M}_{\infty}\!(A)\!)(\tilde{\sigma}^{(N)}_{t}\!-\!\sigma_{t})(\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}(\alpha^{M}_{\infty}\!(B)\!)\!)\Omega_{0})|\!+\!|(\omega^{(N)}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}\!\!-\!\omega^{(N)}_{0,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}})(\alpha^{M}_{N}\!(A)\!\Ad_{U_{N}(t)}(\alpha^{M}_{N}\!(B)\!)\!)|

\displaystyle\!\leq\!\|A\|\|(\tilde{\sigma}^{(N)}_{t}\!-\!\sigma_{t})(\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}(\alpha^{M}_{\infty}\!(B)\!)\!)\|\!+\!|(\omega^{(N)}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}}\!\!-\!\omega^{(N)}_{0,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pm}})(\alpha^{M}_{N}\!(A)\!\Ad_{U_{N}(t)}(\alpha^{M}_{N}\!(B)\!)\!)|

Thus, the result follows from Corollary
